- problem type: generic class/struct/interface instantiation must report when previously distinct function members acquire identical parameter signatures, while valid inherited implementations must remain merged and diagnostic-free.
- root cause: `CfirGenericInstantiationChecker` manually collected own/interface/extend declarations instead of consuming the compiler's substitution/use-site member scopes. It therefore missed ordinary type-reference triggers, static qualifiers, concrete superclass members, and several inferred instantiations; it also treated generic return types as collision triggers and could report a valid implementation as an ambiguity.
- official Cangjie evidence: cjc 1.0.5 reported `sema_generic_instantiation_causes_ambiguous_functions` for explicit, inferred-constructor, static-qualified, and outer-generic-function instantiations, while marker-free `generic_override05.cj` produced no errors. `external/cangjie_compiler/src/Sema/InheritanceChecker/InstantiatedChecker.cpp:56-118,199-280,412-432`, `MergeInheritedMemberHelper.cpp:135-205`, and `StructInheritanceChecker.cpp:358-412` show that inherited members are merged before instantiation and only independent functions whose instantiated parameter types and static status coincide are diagnosed.
- Kotlin counterpart files consulted: `FirClassSubstitutionScope.kt`, `AbstractFirUseSiteMemberScope.kt`, and `FirTypeIntersectionScopeContext.kt` under `external/kotlin/compiler/fir/resolve`.
- CFIR owner files changed: `cfir/checkers/src/org/cangnova/cangjie/cfir/analysis/checkers/declaration/CfirGenericInstantiationChecker.kt`.
- repair principle: derive concrete instantiated members through `CfirClassUseSiteMemberScope` plus `CfirClassSubstitutionScope`, preserving pre-instantiation independent-member identity while letting established override/implementation chains remain merged.
- fixtures covered: `inst_dup_functions001.cj`, `inst_dup_functions002.cj`, `inst_dup_functions003.cj`, `inst_dup_functions004.cj`, `inst_dup_functions005.cj`, `inst_dup_functions006.cj`, `inst_dup_functions010.cj`, and the negative guard `generic_override05.cj`, on both PSI and light-tree paths.
- fixture correction: none.
- verification commands: `.\gradlew.bat :cfir:analysis-tests:test --tests '*GenericsGenerated.testInstDupFunctions006' --tests '*GenericsGenerated.testGenericOverride05' --no-daemon --max-workers=1 --console=plain --no-build-cache --no-configuration-cache`; `.\gradlew.bat :cfir:analysis-tests:test --tests '*GenericsGenerated.testInstDupFunctions*' --tests '*GenericsGenerated.testGenericOverride05' --no-daemon --max-workers=1 --console=plain --no-build-cache --no-configuration-cache`.
- verification outcome: the focused superclass/negative-guard command was `BUILD SUCCESSFUL`; the full 20-test cluster left only PSI/non-PSI `testInstDupFunctions003`, whose expected generic-instantiation and ambiguous-call diagnostics now match exactly and whose sole remaining difference is the separately classified `UNABLE_TO_INFER_RETURN_TYPE` on `bar`. All other 18 selected tests passed.

## Generic static-member completeness and effective property writability

- problem type: generic type arguments must be rejected when their actual interface type leaves required static members unimplemented; abstract `mut static prop` contracts must remain writable through an upper bound; extension implementation obligations must be reported once by the extension owner.
- root cause: CFIR had no incomplete-static-type diagnostic or shared completeness query, treated an abstract mutable property without a local setter body as immutable, and let class-level and extension-level inheritance checkers own the same unimplemented static obligations.
- official Cangjie evidence: cjc 1.0.5 reports only `sema_cannot_instantiated_by_incomplete_type` for `generic_call_static_impl01`, `02`, and `09`; `03` and `04` have no semantic errors; `05` reports one `sema_need_member_implementation` with notes for the missing property and function. Official implementation was traced through `external/cangjie_compiler/src/Sema/TypeCheckGeneric.cpp:59-187`, `TypeCheckExpr/NameReferenceExpr.cpp:681-778`, and `InheritanceChecker/StructInheritanceChecker.cpp:358-412,876-1016,1131-1152`.
- Kotlin counterpart files consulted: `FirReassignmentAndInvisibleSetterChecker.kt`, `FirNotImplementedOverrideChecker.kt`, `FirHelpers.kt` implementation-status logic, and `FirReifiedChecker.kt` for per-type-argument source pairing.
- CFIR owner files changed: `CfirAssignmentLegalityChecker.kt`, new `CfirStaticMemberCompleteness.kt`, `CfirGenericInstantiationChecker.kt`, `CfirInheritanceDeepChecker.kt`, the diagnostic generator/generated/non-suppressible/default-message surfaces, and `DiagnosticNameMapper.kt`.
- repair principle: compute static completeness without reporting, let generic instantiation and inheritance owners consume that model at their own declaration boundaries, and determine property mutability from the language contract independently of accessor implementation bodies.
- fixtures covered: PSI and non-PSI `generic_call_static_impl01.cj`, `02.cj`, `03.cj`, `04.cj`, `05.cj`, and `09.cj`; the wildcard regression also covered the remaining `testGenericCallStaticImpl*` methods.
- fixture correction: `generic_call_static_impl01.cj`, `02.cj`, and `09.cj` now mark the complete `I2` identifier, as required by the repository full-token diagnostic range policy.
- verification command: `.\gradlew.bat :cfir:analysis-tests:test --tests '*GenericsGenerated.testGenericCallStaticImpl*' --no-daemon --max-workers=1 --console=plain --no-build-cache --no-configuration-cache`.
- verification outcome: `BUILD SUCCESSFUL in 1m`; all 26 selected PSI/non-PSI tests passed.

## Generic override return-type invariance and diagnostic collision ownership

- problem type: generic implementations whose direct return type parameter has only interface/generic upper bounds must not use that bound as return covariance; ordinary incompatible override returns must report the concrete incompatibility once.
- root cause: `CfirInheritanceDeepChecker` skipped all return comparisons containing type parameters, so generic invariance errors were absent, while the pending reporter could additionally commit an accessibility error on the same member-name range. The initial direct-bound model also lacked the official transitive upper-bound exposure step.
- official Cangjie evidence: cjc 1.0.5 reports `sema_return_type_invariance` for `generic_override01`, `generic_override02`, and `generic_override06`, and only `sema_return_type_incompatible` for `class.cj` and `class2.cj`. `external/cangjie_compiler/src/Sema/InheritanceChecker/StructInheritanceChecker.cpp:1331-1350,1364-1407` defines the generic-return rule; `external/cangjie_compiler/src/Sema/PreCheck.cpp:1100-1129,1151` exposes transitive generic upper bounds; `external/cangjie_compiler/include/cangjie/Basic/DiagnosticEngine.h:540-570` deduplicates diagnostics by main range and severity.
- Kotlin counterpart files consulted: `external/kotlin/compiler/fir/providers/src/org/jetbrains/kotlin/fir/scopes/impl/FirAbstractOverrideChecker.kt`, `FirStandardOverrideChecker.kt`, and `external/kotlin/compiler/fir/checkers/src/org/jetbrains/kotlin/fir/analysis/checkers/declaration/FirOverrideChecker.kt` for separating substituted override compatibility from declaration diagnostic reporting.
- CFIR owner files changed: `cfir/checkers/src/org/cangnova/cangjie/cfir/analysis/checkers/declaration/CfirInheritanceDeepChecker.kt` and `common/diagnostics/src/org/cangnova/cangjie/cfir/diagnostics/impl/PendingDiagnosticsReporterImpl.kt`; fixture corrections in `generic_override01.cj`, `generic_override02.cj`, `generic_override06.cj`, `class.cj`, and `class2.cj`.
- repair principle: keep substituted return compatibility in the shared inheritance checker, recursively expose concrete class upper bounds before applying generic invariance, and resolve same-range diagnostic ownership centrally before commit.
- fixtures covered: PSI and non-PSI `generic_override01.cj`, `generic_override02.cj`, `generic_override06.cj`, `class.cj`, and `class2.cj`.
- fixture correction: stale `OVERRIDING_RETURN_TYPE_MISMATCH` markers were replaced by the project diagnostics corresponding to the official classifications: `RETURN_TYPE_INVARIANCE` for the three generic cases and `RETURN_TYPE_INCOMPATIBLE` for the two concrete cases.
- verification commands: focused PSI/LT run for the five methods, followed by the complete PSI/LT `GenericsGenerated` suites.
- verification outcome: the focused run was `BUILD SUCCESSFUL in 6m 21s` with all 10 tests passing. The complete regression ran 296 tests with 58 failures, down from 68 before this repair; none of the five repaired methods remained in the failure list.

## Type-parameter upper-bound intersection member resolution

- problem type: member lookup on a type parameter with multiple upper bounds must retain distinct generic instantiations, merge equivalent properties, preserve function/property kind conflicts, and let a target function type disambiguate callable references.
- root cause: receiver scope traversal deduplicated class-like bounds by `ClassId`, losing the second of `I<Rune>` and `I<Int32>`; `CfirCompositeTypeScope` used inconsistent function/property/callable merging; callable-reference atom recovery read an error carrier candidate instead of the real ambiguity payload; non-function expected types incorrectly ran callable-reference compatibility and removed function candidates before mixed-kind ambiguity could form.
- official Cangjie evidence: cjc 1.0.5 accepts `generic_upperbound_reference_01.cj` and `07.cj`, reports exactly two `sema_ambiguous_func_ref` diagnostics for `02.cj`, and reports both `sema_ambiguous_use` and `sema_inherit_super_member_kind_inconsistent` for `06.cj`. Implementation evidence was traced through `external/cangjie_compiler/src/Sema/TypeCheckExpr/NameReferenceExpr.cpp:200-238,327-388,743-777,901-940`, `TypeCheckCall.cpp:1497-1564`, `TypeCheckReference.cpp:376-395,551-573`, and `InheritanceChecker/StructInheritanceChecker.cpp:419-439,752-779`.
- Kotlin counterpart files consulted: `external/kotlin/compiler/fir/providers/src/org/jetbrains/kotlin/fir/resolve/ScopeUtils.kt`, `FirTypeIntersectionScope.kt`, `FirTypeIntersectionScopeContext.kt`, `external/kotlin/compiler/fir/resolve/src/org/jetbrains/kotlin/fir/resolve/calls/FirCallResolver.kt`, `CheckCallableReferenceExpectedType.kt`, and `ConeOverloadConflictResolver.kt`.
- CFIR owner files changed: `cfir/providers/src/org/cangnova/cangjie/cfir/calls/CfirReceivers.kt`, `cfir/providers/src/org/cangnova/cangjie/cfir/scopes/impl/CfirCompositeTypeScope.kt`, `cfir/resolve/src/org/cangnova/cangjie/cfir/resolve/calls/ConeResolutionAtom.kt`, `cfir/resolve/src/org/cangnova/cangjie/cfir/resolve/calls/candidate/CfirCandidateCollector.kt`, `cfir/resolve/src/org/cangnova/cangjie/cfir/resolve/calls/stages/ArgumentCheckingProcessor.kt`, `CfirCheckCallableReferenceExpectedType.kt`, `cfir/resolve/src/org/cangnova/cangjie/cfir/resolve/body/CfirCallResolver.kt`, and the combination-scope call sites in `CfirInheritanceDeepChecker.kt` / `CfirExtendExtraChecker.kt`.
- repair principle: build one intersection-aware candidate surface from complete instantiated upper-bound types, then carry its real candidate set through ordinary callable-reference completion and mixed-kind resolution instead of reconstructing or suppressing candidates at fixture sites.
- fixtures covered: PSI and non-PSI `generic_upperbound_reference_01.cj`, `02.cj`, `06.cj`, and `07.cj`.
- fixture correction: none; `INHERIT_SUPER_MEMBER_KIND_INCONSISTENT` was moved to the shared class-name diagnostic source so its range covers the full `A` token under project policy.
- verification commands: focused runs for 01/02/06/07 during repair, then the complete four-fixture PSI/LT command, followed by both complete `GenericsGenerated` suites.
- verification outcome: the complete focused cluster was `BUILD SUCCESSFUL in 2m 19s`, all 8 PSI/LT tests passed. The full regression ran 296 tests with 50 failures, down from 58 before this repair; none of the four repaired methods remained in the failure list.

## Generic parent-constructor delegation substitution

- problem type: `this(...)` and `super(...)` delegation must resolve constructors through the complete instantiated target type, preserve already-fixed owner substitutions, and reject constructor signatures whose declaration-side parameter type is erroneous.
- root cause: delegation resolution discarded the direct parent type arguments and enumerated raw constructor declarations; after constructors were moved through a substitution scope, the fresh-variable stage still recreated the substituted owner's type parameters because delegation has no explicit receiver. In addition, the general error-type compatibility rule let a declaration parameter containing an undeclared type match every argument before candidate failure could be recorded.
- official Cangjie evidence: cjc 1.0.5 accepts `class_generic_inheritance1.cj` and `class_generic_inheritance_multi.cj`; `generic_parameters1.cj` has two undeclared `T` diagnostics plus an unmatched `super(0)`. `external/cangjie_compiler/src/Sema/TypeCheckReference.cpp:55-69,99-145` derives `super` from the complete direct-parent instance; `TypeCheckCall.cpp:676-691,1311-1347,1365-1387,2260-2285` enumerates parent constructors and checks their substituted signatures.
- Kotlin counterpart files consulted: `external/kotlin/compiler/fir/resolve/src/org/jetbrains/kotlin/fir/resolve/transformers/body/resolve/FirExpressionsResolveTransformer.kt`, `FirCallResolver.kt`, `FirTowerResolver.kt`, `MapTypeArguments.kt`, and `ScopeUtils.kt`.
- CFIR owner files changed: `cfir/resolve/src/org/cangnova/cangjie/cfir/resolve/body/CfirExpressionsResolveTransformer.kt`, `CfirCallResolver.kt`, `cfir/providers/src/org/cangnova/cangjie/cfir/scopes/impl/CfirClassSubstitutionScope.kt`, `cfir/resolve/src/org/cangnova/cangjie/cfir/resolve/calls/stages/CfirCreateFreshTypeVariableSubstitutorStage.kt`, `CfirCheckArguments.kt`, and `cfir/checkers/src/org/cangnova/cangjie/cfir/analysis/diagnostics/coneDiagnosticToCfirDiagnostic.kt`.
- repair principle: constructor delegation carries the complete target type into the ordinary substituted member-scope and candidate pipeline, where fixed owner arguments remain fixed and invalid declaration signatures make the candidate inapplicable before permissive error-type compatibility can select it.
- fixtures covered: PSI and non-PSI `generic_parameters1.cj`, `class_generic_inheritance1.cj`, and `class_generic_inheritance_multi.cj`.
- fixture correction: none.
- verification command: `.\gradlew.bat :cfir:analysis-tests:test --tests '*GenericsGenerated.testGenericParameters1' --tests '*GenericsGenerated.testClassGenericInheritance1' --tests '*GenericsGenerated.testClassGenericInheritanceMulti' --no-daemon --max-workers=1 --console=plain --no-build-cache --no-configuration-cache`.
- verification outcome: `BUILD SUCCESSFUL in 8m 9s`; all 6 PSI/LT tests passed. The subsequent complete PSI/LT `GenericsGenerated` regression ran 296 tests with 45 failures, down from 50 before this repair; the three repaired methods remained green.

## Callable-reference completion with outer inference variables

- problem type: callable references used as arguments of an outer generic call must contribute constraints while the outer variables are unfixed, and a structural qualifier error must own its range without an additional derived no-match diagnostic.
- root cause: the callable-reference post-completion path performed a final nominal subtype test against an expected function type that still contained the outer call's unfixed variables, discarding valid Unit, explicitly instantiated, and bound-member references. Its failure mapper also inspected only an explicit receiver, missing the case where the failed argument itself was the erroneous bare generic qualifier.
- official Cangjie evidence: the previously established cjc 1.0.5 evidence for `instantiate_02.cj` requires filtering only after the resulting substitution is available; the function-composition fixtures are accepted, while `generic_call_static_impl11.cj` has only the bare-generic-classifier error.
- Kotlin counterpart files consulted: `external/kotlin/compiler/fir/resolve/src/org/jetbrains/kotlin/fir/resolve/calls/CheckCallableReferenceExpectedType.kt`, `FirCallResolver.kt`, and the FIR postponed-argument completion path.
- CFIR owner files changed: `cfir/resolve/src/org/cangnova/cangjie/cfir/resolve/body/CfirCallResolver.kt` and `cfir/checkers/src/org/cangnova/cangjie/cfir/analysis/diagnostics/coneDiagnosticToCfirDiagnostic.kt`.
- repair principle: postpone final nominal filtering while either side depends on outer unfixed variables, retain the resulting-substitutor filter once variables are fixed, and suppress a derived callable-reference no-match when the argument/qualifier subtree already carries the structural primary error.
- fixtures covered: PSI and non-PSI `function19_unitRetType_test1.cj`, `function19_unitRetType_test2.cj`, `function20_unitRetType_test1.cj`, `function20_unitRetType_test3.cj`, and `generic_call_static_impl11.cj`; guards included `instantiate_02.cj`, `instantiation_test.cj`, and `callInference11/12`.
- fixture correction: none.
- verification commands: the combined 14-test PSI/LT target-and-guard command, followed by focused PSI/LT `generic_call_static_impl11`, `instantiate_02`, and `function19_unitRetType_test1`.
- verification outcome: the first run passed 12/14 and isolated only the qualifier cascade; after the shared ownership fix the focused rerun was `BUILD SUCCESSFUL in 2m 51s`. The subsequent complete Generics regression no longer contained any of the five repaired methods.

## Post-instantiation member-signature collisions after inherited-member merging

- problem type: generic instantiation must diagnose only parameter-signature collisions newly created by substitution, after inherited requirements have been semantically merged, and inferred constructor calls must retain the finalized instantiated type for this checker.
- root cause: the checker expanded direct-overridden declarations after consuming the use-site/substitution scope, splitting merged inherited provenance into duplicate members. The provider also lacked a precise repeated-path merge key, while failed constructor expressions discarded the candidate constraint-system substitution and emitted a derived inference error beside the instantiation collision.
- official Cangjie evidence: cjc 1.0.5 reports only `sema_lambdaExpr_must_have_type_annotation` for `generic_subst_perf.cj`, and exactly `sema_generic_instantiation_causes_ambiguous_functions` for `A(0)` in `err_ambiguous.cj`. `external/cangjie_compiler/src/Sema/InheritanceChecker/InstantiatedChecker.cpp:56-115,201-256,412-433`, `StructInheritanceChecker.cpp:358-439`, and `MergeInheritedMemberHelper.cpp:135-205` establish merge-before-substitution and parameter-only collision checking.
- Kotlin counterpart files consulted: `FirClassSubstitutionScope.kt`, `AbstractFirUseSiteMemberScope.kt`, `FirTypeIntersectionScopeContext.kt`, and `FirImplementationMismatchChecker.kt` under `external/kotlin/compiler/fir`.
- CFIR owner files changed: `cfir/providers/src/org/cangnova/cangjie/cfir/scopes/impl/CfirClassUseSiteMemberScope.kt`, `cfir/checkers/src/org/cangnova/cangjie/cfir/analysis/checkers/declaration/CfirGenericInstantiationChecker.kt`, `cfir/semantics/src/org/cangnova/cangjie/cfir/semantics/AbstractCallCandidate.kt`, `cfir/resolve/src/org/cangnova/cangjie/cfir/resolve/calls/candidate/Candidate.kt`, and `cfir/checkers/src/org/cangnova/cangjie/cfir/analysis/collectors/components/ErrorNodeDiagnosticCollectorComponent.kt`.
- repair principle: merge only repeated inherited paths whose direct provenance signature and instantiated signature are both equal, preserve independent declarations for substitution-time collision checking, and expose finalized candidate substitutions read-only to the checker without feeding the diagnostic back into candidate applicability.
- fixtures covered: PSI and non-PSI `generic_subst_perf.cj`, `err_ambiguous.cj`, all `inst_dup_functions001.cj` through `inst_dup_functions010.cj`, `generic_override05.cj`, and upper-bound overload guard `generic_upperbound_reference_03.cj`.
- fixture correction: `generic_subst_perf.cj` now marks the independently untyped lambda parameter with project diagnostic `LAMBDA_MUST_HAVE_TYPE_ANNOTATION`, as required by cjc 1.0.5.
- verification commands: focused PSI/LT target runs; target plus `inst_dup_functions006` / `generic_override05`; complete `testInstDupFunctions*` family; upper-bound regression command; then both complete PSI/LT `GenericsGenerated` suites.
- verification outcome: targets and guards passed; the 24-test instantiation family retained only the separately classified `UNABLE_TO_INFER_RETURN_TYPE` difference in `inst_dup_functions003` while every instantiation-collision marker matched. The final complete regression ran 296 tests with 35 failures, down from 49 at the start of this turn; `generic_subst_perf`, `err_ambiguous`, and `generic_upperbound_reference_03` were green on both PSI and light-tree paths.

## Type-parameter classifier recognition with explicit type arguments

- problem type: a type parameter followed by explicit type arguments remains a resolved type-parameter classifier and must report generic-argument arity mismatch instead of undeclared type.
- root cause: `CfirTypeResolver.resolveUserType` only queried the type-parameter scope when the user type had no explicit type arguments, so `R<R>` / `T<T>` fell through to class-like lookup and became `UNDECLARED_TYPE_NAME`.
- official Cangjie evidence: cjc 1.0.5 reports `sema_generic_argument_no_match` for `class13.cj` and `interface3.cj`. `external/cangjie_compiler/src/Sema/PreCheck.cpp:394-447,614-640`, `TypeCheckType.cpp:196-217`, and `TypeCheckGeneric.cpp:214-236` bind `GENERIC_PARAM_DECL` as a legal type target before comparing its zero declared generic parameters with supplied arguments.
- Kotlin counterpart files consulted: `external/kotlin/compiler/fir/resolve/src/org/jetbrains/kotlin/fir/resolve/providers/impl/FirTypeResolverImpl.kt`, where a `FirTypeParameterSymbol` is resolved first and explicit arguments produce the dedicated unexpected-type-arguments error.
- CFIR owner files changed: `cfir/resolve/src/org/cangnova/cangjie/cfir/resolve/CfirTypeResolver.kt` and `cfir/checkers/src/org/cangnova/cangjie/cfir/analysis/checkers/declaration/CfirSupertypesChecker.kt`.
- repair principle: classifier identity is resolved independently from generic-argument validation; preserve the nominal outer classifier and nested error arguments for scope/substitution, while supertype legality separately evaluates the complete type tree.
- fixtures covered: PSI and non-PSI `class13.cj`, `interface3.cj`, and regression guard `generic_parameters8.cj`.
- fixture correction: `class13.cj` moves `CLASS_UNINITIALIZED_FIELD` to the field-name token under the project range policy.
- verification commands: combined seven-method PSI/LT run; focused `class13` / `interface3`; then `generic_parameters8`, `enum6_test`, and `function14_test`; finally both complete PSI/LT `GenericsGenerated` suites.
- verification outcome: `class13` and `interface3` passed 4/4; after preserving invalid nested-supertype legality, `generic_parameters8`, `enum6_test`, and `function14_test` passed 6/6. The final complete Generics regression ran 296 tests with 24 failures.

## Named-parameter shape preflight and invalid-call propagation

- problem type: positional arguments targeting named-only parameters must be rejected before value-expression resolution; the failed call must propagate an invalid result so argument, selector, and return-inference cascades are suppressed.
- root cause: CFIR resolved every argument value before parameter mapping, `CfirMapArguments` did not stop later stages after mapping errors, error references leaked the candidate's nominal result type, and error-typed arguments were accepted by ordinary compatibility before an invalid-call diagnostic could propagate.
- official Cangjie evidence: cjc 1.0.5 reports only three type-position `sema_undeclared_type_name` diagnostics and `sema_need_named_argument` for `enum6_test.cj`; `function14_test.cj` retains calls to a declaration with erroneous parameter/return types. `external/cangjie_compiler/src/Sema/TypeCheckCall.cpp:618-646,1784-1821,2295-2304,2328-2475,2909-2937`, `TypeCheckExpr/NameReferenceExpr.cpp:291-309`, `TypeCheckUtil.cpp:228-231`, and `TypeChecker.cpp:92-123,2728-2774` show shape-first mapping, scoped candidate-diagnostic replay, InvalidTy propagation, and cascade suppression.
- Kotlin counterpart files consulted: FIR `ResolutionStages.kt` / argument-mapping stages, `ArgumentCheckingProcessor.kt`, `TypeCheckerStateForConstraintSystem.kt`, `ConstraintInjector.kt`, `coneDiagnosticToFirDiagnostic.kt`, and `FirTypeResolverImpl.kt`.
- CFIR owner files changed: `cfir/resolve/src/org/cangnova/cangjie/cfir/resolve/calls/stages/CfirMapArguments.kt`, `CfirCheckArguments.kt`, `ArgumentCheckingProcessor.kt`, `cfir/resolve/src/org/cangnova/cangjie/cfir/resolve/ResolveUtils.kt`, `cfir/resolve/src/org/cangnova/cangjie/cfir/resolve/inference/CfirCallCompleter.kt`, `cfir/resolve/src/org/cangnova/cangjie/cfir/resolve/calls/ResolutionContext.kt`, `ResolutionStageRunner.kt`, `cfir/resolve/src/org/cangnova/cangjie/cfir/resolve/body/CfirCallResolver.kt`, `CfirExpressionsResolveTransformer.kt`, `CfirDeclarationsResolveTransformer.kt`, and `CfirTypeResolver.kt`.
- repair principle: run an isolated candidate shape probe before value arguments, commit it only when every final best candidate fails solely with `NeedNamedArgument`, resolve only nested type references in the skipped argument, and propagate structured error results through outer calls without publishing derived mismatch diagnostics.
- fixtures covered: PSI and non-PSI `enum6_test.cj`, `function14_test.cj`, `generic_parameters1.cj`, plus complete Generics regression.
- fixture correction: five fixtures replaced eight obsolete type-position `UNRESOLVED_REFERENCE` markers with cjc-backed `UNDECLARED_TYPE_NAME`; `function14_test.cj` aligns missing-argument ranges with FIR `VALUE_ARGUMENTS` and the project full-token/range policy.
- verification commands: repeated focused PSI/LT runs for `enum6_test`, `function14_test`, and `generic_parameters1`; focused `generic_parameters8` regression; then both complete PSI/LT `GenericsGenerated` suites.
- verification outcome: the focused target/guard set passed 6/6, the regression set passed 6/6, and the final complete regression ran 296 tests with 24 failures, down from 35 before this repair; no new failures remained.

## Generic enum owner/member type-argument ownership and recoverable pattern flow

- problem type: generic enum owner qualifiers, bare enum-constructor sugar, and enum members with their own written type arguments must use distinct type-argument channels; a qualified-member legality error must preserve the instantiated enum result and must not make later patterns unreachable.
- root cause: classifier-as-receiver fallback constructed nominal types without validating the classifier's own arity; `CfirMapTypeArguments` treated member-written type arguments as owner arguments even when an explicit enum qualifier had already supplied the owner; bare-generic diagnostics were then reported again on the enum member. Independently, the unreachable-pattern checker propagated known initializer values from global variables although its model is only sound for local values.
- official Cangjie evidence: cjc 1.0.5 reports `sema_generic_argument_no_match` for `TimeUnit2<Float64, Int32>.Year2`, `sema_generic_type_without_type_argument` for `TimeUnit2.Year2`, and only `sema_invalid_type_param_of_enum_member_access` for `TimeUnit<Int32>.Year<Int64>(4)`; the latter keeps both `Year` and `Month` patterns reachable. Bare `Year<Int32>(4)` is accepted. `external/cangjie_compiler/src/Sema/TypeCheckReference.cpp:254-278,449-492,667-710`, `EnumSugarChecker.cpp:43-91`, `TypeCheckUtil.cpp:680-717`, `TypeCheckDecl.cpp:468-525`, and `TypeCheckPattern.cpp:367-403` establish qualifier arity checking, the separate bare-sugar owner substitution, non-destructive member legality reporting, enum result typing, and pattern matching from the instantiated selector type.
- Kotlin counterpart files consulted: `external/kotlin/compiler/fir/resolve/src/org/jetbrains/kotlin/fir/resolve/calls/stages/TypeArgumentMapping.kt`, `CreateFreshTypeVariableSubstitutorStage.kt`, `external/kotlin/compiler/fir/resolve/src/org/jetbrains/kotlin/fir/resolve/providers/impl/FirTypeResolverImpl.kt`, and `external/kotlin/compiler/fir/checkers/src/org/jetbrains/kotlin/fir/analysis/checkers/expression/FirPropertyAccessTypeArgumentsChecker.kt`.
- CFIR owner files changed: `cfir/resolve/src/org/cangnova/cangjie/cfir/resolve/ResolveUtils.kt`, `cfir/resolve/src/org/cangnova/cangjie/cfir/resolve/calls/stages/CfirMapTypeArguments.kt`, `cfir/checkers/src/org/cangnova/cangjie/cfir/analysis/checkers/expression/CfirGenericBareClassifierAccessChecker.kt`, `CfirMatchUnreachablePatternChecker.kt`, `cfir/checkers/src/org/cangnova/cangjie/cfir/analysis/checkers/CommonExpressionCheckers.kt`, and the diagnostic generator/generated/default-message surfaces.
- repair principle: validate type arguments at the declaration that owns them, keep bare enum sugar as the only member-spelling path that can instantiate owner parameters, report qualified enum-member type arguments as a non-destructive legality diagnostic, and restrict initializer-based pattern value narrowing to local variables.
- fixtures covered: PSI and non-PSI `enum1.cj`, `enum5.cj`, `enum6.cj`, and `enum7.cj`; guards included `enum19_generic_staticFunc_newTypeParam.cj`, `autobox_match2.cj`, and `unbox_enumPattern.cj`.
- fixture correction: `enum1.cj` now ranges owner arity mismatch over the erroneous qualifier `TimeUnit2<Float64, Int32>` rather than the following member; `enum5.cj` ranges the dedicated diagnostic over the complete owner qualifier `TimeUnit<Int32>`, following the project diagnostic-range policy instead of cjc's single-character anchor.
- verification commands: focused PSI/LT runs for `testEnum1`, `testEnum5`, `testEnum6`, and `testEnum7`; a guard run including generic static-member and local initializer-narrowing cases; then both complete PSI/LT `GenericsGenerated` suites.
- verification outcome: the focused target-and-guard command was `BUILD SUCCESSFUL in 1m 18s` with all 8 tests passing. `testEnum19GenericStaticFuncNewTypeParam` passed, and `autobox_match2` retained its expected local-variable `UNREACHABLE_PATTERN` result while remaining red only on separately classified diagnostics. The final complete Generics regression ran 296 tests with 20 failures, down from 24; `enum1` and `enum5` were green on both PSI and light-tree paths.

## Type-parameter classifier identity in value position

- problem type: a type parameter written in an ordinary value-expression position must remain resolved to its classifier symbol and real type, then report `REF_NOT_BE_TYPE` non-destructively instead of degrading to `UNRESOLVED_REFERENCE` and causing derived type/call mismatches.
- root cause: `CfirCallResolver` searched tower type parameters only when a bare name was used as a receiver. The same name in a standalone value position therefore fell through unresolved-name recovery, so later assignment and call checking consumed an error type rather than the actual type parameter.
- official Cangjie evidence: cjc 1.0.5 reports exactly three `sema_ref_not_be_type` diagnostics for the value-position `T` occurrences in `class4_test.cj`, while the `T` inside `foo<T>` remains a valid type argument; the second write to `width` independently reports `sema_cannot_assign_to_immutable`. `external/cangjie_compiler/src/Sema/TypeCheckExpr/TypeCheckReference.cpp:624-631`, `NameReferenceExpr.cpp:68-82,329-334`, and `InitializationChecker.cpp:165-170,675-687,862-895` preserve classifier identity while separating value-position legality from assignment legality.
- Kotlin counterpart files consulted: `external/kotlin/compiler/fir/resolve/src/org/jetbrains/kotlin/fir/resolve/calls/tower/TowerLevels.kt`, `calls/stages/ResolutionStages.kt`, `transformers/FirCallCompletionResultsWriterTransformer.kt`, and `external/kotlin/compiler/fir/checkers/src/org/jetbrains/kotlin/fir/analysis/checkers/expression/FirTypeParameterInQualifiedAccessChecker.kt`, `FirReassignmentAndInvisibleSetterChecker.kt`, plus `coneDiagnosticToFirDiagnostic.kt`.
- CFIR owner files changed: `cfir/resolve/src/org/cangnova/cangjie/cfir/resolve/body/CfirCallResolver.kt` and `cfir/checkers/src/org/cangnova/cangjie/cfir/analysis/checkers/expression/CfirClassifierAsExpressionChecker.kt`.
- repair principle: resolve classifier identity independently from expression-position legality, preserve the type parameter's symbol and nominal type for downstream recovery, and let the shared qualified-access checker own the non-destructive diagnostic.
- fixtures covered: PSI and non-PSI `class4_test.cj`.
- fixture correction: `class4_test.cj` now ranges `CANNOT_ASSIGN_TO_IMMUTABLE` over the complete left-value token `width`; Kotlin FIR reports reassignment on `expression.lValue.source`, and ordinary assignment fixtures in this repository use the same full-left-value range.
- verification commands: `.\gradlew.bat :cfir:analysis-tests:test --tests 'org.cangnova.cangjie.cfir.analysis.tests.CfirAnalysisLLTTest.llt.generics.GenericsGenerated.testClass4Test' --console=plain`; `.\gradlew.bat :cfir:analysis-tests:test --tests 'org.cangnova.cangjie.cfir.analysis.tests.CfirAnalysisLLTPsiTest.llt.generics.GenericsGenerated.testClass4Test' --console=plain`.
- verification outcome: both focused commands were `BUILD SUCCESSFUL`; light-tree and PSI variants passed independently.

## Classifier values and bare function-reference mismatch ownership

- problem type: class-like values used under an assignment expected type and bare function names used as incompatible constructor arguments must report the official general type mismatch diagnostics, while the failed explicit generic constructor call owns one outer parameters/arguments mismatch.
- root cause: the standalone-classifier checker reported `REF_NOT_BE_TYPE` even when assignment expected typing already owned the incompatibility; the assignment and argument mappers selected internal context-specific mismatch names; the explicit generic constructor failure lacked its call-level diagnostic.
- official Cangjie evidence: cjc 1.0.5 reports exactly `sema_mismatched_types` for `width = C`, `sema_mismatched_types` for `getHeight`, and `sema_parameters_and_arguments_mismatch` for the `Data<(Int64) -> Int64>(...)` call in `class5_test.cj`, with no `sema_ref_not_be_type`. Evidence was traced through `TypeCheckReference.cpp:624-631`, `NameReferenceExpr.cpp:68-82,329-334`, `AssignExpr.cpp:329-391`, `TypeCheckCall.cpp:302-318,676-685,1002-1062,1165-1180`, and `InitializationChecker.cpp:165-170,675-687,862-895`.
- Kotlin counterpart files consulted: FIR assignment expected-type checking, argument mismatch mapping, callable-reference completion, and diagnostic positioning in `FirAssignmentTypeMismatchChecker.kt`, `FirReassignmentAndInvisibleSetterChecker.kt`, call resolution stages, and `coneDiagnosticToFirDiagnostic.kt`.
- CFIR owner files changed: `CfirClassifierAsExpressionChecker.kt`, `CfirAssignmentTypeMismatchChecker.kt`, `coneDiagnosticToCfirDiagnostic.kt`, the `PARAMETERS_AND_ARGUMENTS_MISMATCH` diagnostic surfaces, and `class5_test.cj`.
- repair principle: expected-type and call-candidate owners report the primary incompatibility once, while classifier legality stays non-destructive and does not duplicate an expected-type mismatch.
- fixtures covered: PSI and non-PSI `class5_test.cj`.
- fixture correction: the outer diagnostic covers the complete classifier token `Data`, not cjc's single-character anchor or the following explicit type arguments.
- verification commands: focused LT and PSI `testClass5Test`, followed by both complete `GenericsGenerated` suites.
- verification outcome: both focused variants passed; the final complete regression did not contain `testClass5Test`.

## Generic infinite-instantiation source ownership

- problem type: expanding generic substitution cycles must report on the declaration edge that owns the cycle, while a concrete root construction processed before declarations may transfer ownership to the first expanding member edge.
- root cause: the cycle detector was correct, but `InstantiationContext.source` either stayed permanently on the root constructor call or, after an initial repair, switched on every cross-declaration edge and moved established declaration-graph diagnostics to intermediate edges.
- official Cangjie evidence: cjc 1.0.5 reports only `sema_generic_infinite_instantiation`; `generic_infinite_init_03.cj` reports on `B<A<T>>` even without the `main` construction. `MultiTypeSubstUtils.cpp:470-484`, `InstantiatedChecker.cpp:141-231,262-295`, and `StructInheritanceChecker.cpp:345-355` define expanding substitution and declaration-level traversal.
- Kotlin counterpart files consulted: `FirNonExpansiveInheritanceRestrictionChecker.kt` for the finite graph/expansive-edge architecture.
- CFIR owner files changed: `CfirGenericInstantiationChecker.kt`.
- repair principle: declaration-graph contexts preserve their outer cycle-entry source; only a concrete construction root explicitly marks that the current expanding trigger may take source ownership across a target transition.
- fixtures covered: PSI and non-PSI `generic_infinite_init.cj` and `generic_infinite_init_02.cj` through `generic_infinite_init_07.cj`.
- fixture correction: none.
- verification command: `.\gradlew.bat :cfir:analysis-tests:test --tests '*GenericsGenerated.testGenericInfiniteInit*' --console=plain`, plus the later combined and complete Generics regressions.
- verification outcome: after narrowing the root-call condition, all 14 PSI/LT family tests passed and no generic-infinite method remained in the 296-test regression.

## Recursive constructor dependencies in default parameter values

- problem type: constructor calls evaluated in constructor default parameter expressions participate in the same recursive-constructor dependency graph as field initializers and constructor bodies.
- root cause: `CfirRecursiveConstructorCallChecker` collected owner initializer calls, constructor body calls, and implicit super dependencies, but omitted `constructor.valueParameters[*].defaultValue`.
- official Cangjie evidence: cjc 1.0.5 reports one `sema_recursive_constructor_call` on `init` for `init(a!: A<T> = A<T>())`, with dependency notes pointing to `A<T>` and back to `init`; removing generics preserves the result. Official graph traversal is in `Utils.cpp:42-63,81-214,543-560` and runs after body checking via `TypeCheckClassLike.cpp:127-128`.
- Kotlin counterpart files consulted: `FirCommonConstructorDelegationIssuesChecker.kt` for constructor-symbol DFS and cycle ownership.
- CFIR owner files changed: `CfirRecursiveConstructorCallChecker.kt` and `class_generic_recursive_function_call01.cj`.
- repair principle: default parameter expressions are direct-evaluation dependency edges in the existing constructor graph, not a separate checker or body fallback.
- fixtures covered: PSI and non-PSI `class_generic_recursive_function_call01.cj` and `class_generic_recursive_function_call02.cj`.
- fixture correction: removed the second `RECURSIVE_CONSTRUCTOR_CALL` marker from the default-value call because official output represents it as a dependency note, not another primary error.
- verification command: `.\gradlew.bat :cfir:analysis-tests:test --tests '*GenericsGenerated.testClassGenericRecursiveFunctionCall*' --console=plain`.
- verification outcome: `BUILD SUCCESSFUL`; all four PSI/LT tests passed.

## Static property accessor mutability context

- problem type: static property getter/setter bodies in a struct must not be classified as immutable instance-member contexts when they access the struct's static fields.
- root cause: `CfirPropertyAccessor` is a `CfirFunction`, but its local status does not inherit the owning property's static bit; the struct mutation checker therefore treated a static setter as a non-mut instance function and reported `CANNOT_MODIFY_VAR` on `_v = v`.
- official Cangjie evidence: cjc 1.0.5 reports no semantic error for `in_package_inst_1.cj`; `Data<Int64>` satisfies `IA`, the static setter may update `_v`, and `E<Data<Int64>>.sFoo()` is valid.
- Kotlin counterpart files consulted: FIR/CFIR `BodyResolveContext.withPropertyAccessor` and `FirReassignmentAndInvisibleSetterChecker.kt`.
- CFIR owner files changed: `CfirMutabilityCheckers.kt`.
- repair principle: classify accessor mutation context from the owning property's static contract, keeping getter/setter and ordinary static functions outside instance mutability propagation without changing static scope or completeness.
- fixtures covered: PSI and non-PSI `in_package_inst_1.cj`; guards included `StringGenerated.testString1` command companions and the complete Generics suite, with the existing static-completeness family remaining green.
- fixture correction: none.
- verification outcome: both `testInPackageInst1` variants passed in the combined regression and remained absent from the complete Generics failure list.

## String interpolation ToString legality

- problem type: every non-primitive resolved interpolation part must satisfy `core.ToString`; inference constraints and final legality diagnostics must remain separate owners.
- root cause: resolve added the interpolation subtype constraint but ignored a final incompatibility, while the checker framework had no dedicated `CfirStringInterpolationChecker` slot and no `INVALID_STRING_IMPLEMENTATION` diagnostic surface.
- official Cangjie evidence: cjc 1.0.5 reports exactly one `sema_invalid_string_implementation` on the `{` of `${instance}` for unconstrained `T`; `T <: ToString` and primitive interpolation are accepted. Official checking/lowering is in `LitConstExpr.cpp:131-175` and `StrInterpolationExpr.cpp:75-106`.
- Kotlin counterpart files consulted: `FirExpressionsResolveTransformer.transformStringConcatenationCall`, the generated `FirStringConcatenationCallChecker` slot, and `RedundantSingleExpressionStringTemplateChecker`.
- CFIR owner files changed: checker generator and generated expression components, new `CfirStringInterpolationToStringChecker.kt`, `CommonExpressionCheckers.kt`, and the diagnostic generator/generated/default-message/mapper surfaces.
- repair principle: keep interpolation constraints in resolve for inference, then validate each final resolved part in one shared checker with a common PSI/LT source-positioning path.
- fixtures covered: PSI and non-PSI `generic_string_interpolation.cj` plus `StringGenerated.testString1` and primitive/ToString/PCLA guards.
- fixture correction: none.
- verification outcome: both target variants and the string guard passed in the combined 26-test regression; no interpolation failure remained in the complete Generics run.

## Static lambda generic-dependency diagnostic multiplicity

- problem type: the static-member generic dependency legality pass reports once for every dependent AST node in a lambda, not once for the enclosing static field initializer.
- root cause: `function20_unitRetType_test2.cj` expected only the two outer-lambda diagnostics although CFIR already matched the official six-node diagnostic surface.
- official Cangjie evidence: cjc 1.0.5 reports `sema_static_variable_use_generic_parameter` on each full lambda, its parameter type `T`, and body reference `input`, for six total diagnostics across fields `a` and `b`. `LegalityOfUsage.cpp:68-149`, `Diags.cpp:637-651`, and `DiagnosticSema.def:134` show the independent AST walker reports.
- Kotlin counterpart files consulted: none; this is Cangjie-specific legality semantics.
- CFIR owner files changed: fixture only, `function20_unitRetType_test2.cj`.
- repair principle: preserve the non-destructive per-node legality pass and correct the stale expected surface instead of suppressing valid nested diagnostics.
- fixtures covered: PSI and non-PSI `function20_unitRetType_test2.cj`.
- fixture correction: added nested markers for `T` and `input` inside each already-marked lambda.
- verification outcome: both variants passed in the combined regression and remained green in the complete Generics suite.

## Invalid generic upper bounds and explicit-candidate nominal recovery

- problem type: non-class/interface explicit declaration bounds must be diagnosed at the declaration, while an explicitly instantiated constructor that fails a valid upper-bound constraint must retain its substituted nominal result type for downstream member access and type propagation.
- root cause: declaration-bound legality, fresh-variable constraint initialization, and use-site upper-bound checking did not share one legality rule, so primitive invalid bounds entered the constraint system; failed candidates then collapsed to `ConeErrorType`, losing the substituted `Aoo<Bool>` return type and causing derived member/type cascades after `Aoo<Bool>(false)`.
- official Cangjie evidence: cjc 1.0.5 reports the declaration-side upper-bound legality error on type parameter `T`, a generic type-argument constraint error on the explicit `Bool` use, and no derived error for the later `a.a` access. `external/cangjie_compiler/include/cangjie/Basic/DiagRefactor/DiagnosticSema.def:98-99`, `external/cangjie_compiler/src/Sema/PreCheck.cpp:986-1050,1148-1149`, and `external/cangjie_compiler/src/Sema/TypeCheckGeneric.cpp:214-272` define declaration-bound legality and use-site instantiation checks.
- Kotlin counterpart files consulted: `external/kotlin/compiler/fir/resolve/src/org/jetbrains/kotlin/fir/resolve/calls/stages/CreateFreshTypeVariableSubstitutorStage.kt`, `external/kotlin/compiler/fir/checkers/src/org/jetbrains/kotlin/fir/analysis/checkers/FirUpperBoundViolatedHelpers.kt`, `FirUpperBoundViolatedTypeChecker.kt`, `FirUpperBoundViolatedQualifiedAccessExpressionChecker.kt`, `external/kotlin/compiler/fir/resolve/src/org/jetbrains/kotlin/fir/resolve/ResolveUtils.kt`, and `FirCallCompletionResultsWriterTransformer.kt`.
- CFIR owner files changed: `cfir/providers/src/org/cangnova/cangjie/cfir/types/TypeUtils.kt`, `cfir/resolve/src/org/cangnova/cangjie/cfir/resolve/calls/stages/CfirCreateFreshTypeVariableSubstitutorStage.kt`, `cfir/checkers/src/org/cangnova/cangjie/cfir/analysis/checkers/CfirUpperBoundViolatedHelpers.kt`, `cfir/resolve/src/org/cangnova/cangjie/cfir/resolve/ResolveUtils.kt`, and `cfir/resolve/src/org/cangnova/cangjie/cfir/resolve/inference/CfirCallCompletionResultsWriterTransformer.kt`.
- repair principle: centralize declaration-bound legality for constraint initialization and diagnostics, then recover only the explicitly substituted candidate return type at the shared `typeFromCallee` seam when the explicit type arguments caused a constraint contradiction, without hiding the root diagnostic or generalizing error types.
- fixtures covered: PSI and non-PSI `err_invalid_upper.cj`.
- fixture correction: moved `UPPER_BOUND_MUST_BE_CLASS_OR_INTERFACE` to type parameter `T`, and replaced the broad constructor marker with `GENERIC_TYPE_ARGUMENT_NOT_MATCH_CONSTRAINT` on the explicit `Bool` type argument.
- verification commands: `.\gradlew.bat :cfir:analysis-tests:test --tests '*GenericsGenerated.testErrInvalidUpper' --tests '*GenericsGenerated.testClass5Test' --no-daemon --max-workers=1 --console=plain`; the four-method critical Generics regression; then both complete PSI/LT `GenericsGenerated` suites.
- verification outcome: the focused target and guard passed; the critical regression passed 8/8; the complete regression passed 296/296.

## Ambiguous classifier type uses and erroneous callable-signature propagation

- problem type: classifier redeclarations must produce full type-use ambiguity and inheritance diagnostics, while erroneous callable parameter signatures must distinguish ordinary undeclared types from redeclaration ambiguity when deciding whether a call-level no-match cascade is official.
- root cause: type resolution did not consume the complete classifier conflict set, ambiguity sources covered only the classifier name, redeclared constructors leaked a derived constructor ambiguity, and argument checking represented erroneous arguments, ordinary erroneous signatures, and ambiguous classifier signatures with the same resolution diagnostic.
- official Cangjie evidence: cjc 1.0.5 produces 15 diagnostics for `generic_parameters2.cj`: three classifier redeclarations, six complete type-use ambiguities, one undeclared `Z`, four class-inherits-non-class/interface errors, and one `foo` call no-match, with no constructor ambiguity for `B<Int32, Int64>()` or `C<Int32, Int64>()`. The same compiler continues recovery through `test2(1)` in `function14_test.cj`, whose parameter and return types are undeclared `T`, without a call no-match. Implementation evidence was traced through `external/cangjie_compiler/src/Sema/PreCheck.cpp:226-265,386-451`, `TypeCheckClassLike.cpp:137-164`, `TypeCheckCall.cpp:2583-2597`, and `Diagnostic/Diags.cpp:138-160,452-470`.
- Kotlin counterpart files consulted: `external/kotlin/compiler/fir/resolve/src/org/jetbrains/kotlin/fir/resolve/FirTypeResolver.kt`, `providers/impl/FirTypeResolverImpl.kt`, `calls/stages/ArgumentCheckingProcessor.kt`, `external/kotlin/compiler/fir/semantics/src/org/jetbrains/kotlin/fir/resolve/calls/ResolutionDiagnostic.kt`, `external/kotlin/compiler/fir/checkers/src/org/jetbrains/kotlin/fir/analysis/diagnostics/coneDiagnosticToFirDiagnostic.kt`, and `FirSupertypesChecker.kt`.
- CFIR owner files changed: `cfir/resolve/src/org/cangnova/cangjie/cfir/resolve/CfirTypeResolver.kt`, `cfir/semantics/src/org/cangnova/cangjie/cfir/diagnostic/ConeDiagnostic.kt`, `cfir/semantics/src/org/cangnova/cangjie/cfir/semantics/ResolutionDiagnostic.kt`, `cfir/resolve/src/org/cangnova/cangjie/cfir/resolve/calls/stages/ArgumentCheckingProcessor.kt`, `cfir/checkers/src/org/cangnova/cangjie/cfir/analysis/diagnostics/coneDiagnosticToCfirDiagnostic.kt`, `cfir/checkers/src/org/cangnova/cangjie/cfir/analysis/checkers/declaration/CfirSupertypesChecker.kt`, and the inheritance-diagnostic generator/generated/default/non-suppressible/name-mapping surfaces.
- repair principle: resolve classifier type uses from the shared conflict tracker with a complete source, preserve one structured ambiguity through the type tree, and split erroneous arguments, ordinary erroneous signatures, and classifier-ambiguity signatures so only the official ambiguity case produces call no-match and constructor cascades remain suppressed.
- fixtures covered: PSI and non-PSI `generic_parameters2.cj`, with `function14_test.cj` as the ordinary erroneous-signature recovery guard.
- fixture correction: updated `generic_parameters2.cj` to the official 15-diagnostic surface with complete type-use ranges and the single `foo` no-match; `function14_test.cj` required no correction.
- verification commands: the four-method critical Generics regression selecting `testGenericParameters2` and `testFunction14Test` together with `testClass3` and `testFunction1`; then both complete PSI/LT `GenericsGenerated` suites.
- verification outcome: the critical regression passed 8/8; the complete regression passed 296/296.

## Target-typed overloaded function references in generic enum payload calls

- problem type: overloaded function names used as payload arguments of a generic enum constructor must be resolved inside each outer candidate constraint-system branch, including explicit function type arguments, generic-overload exclusion, and outer argument-type ambiguity.
- root cause: callable-reference arguments required a pre-fixed `ConeFunctionType`, resolved candidates outside the outer enum candidate system, failed to register imported fresh variables for completion, and attached an extra generic `InapplicableCandidate` after the specific callable-reference failure. Enum declaration-self receivers also produced the invalid identity owner substitution `T -> T`, so the outer owner parameter was not freshened. Completion then regenerated a callee-level `NO_CONSTRUCTOR` from the propagated argument failure.
- official Cangjie evidence: cjc 1.0.5 accepts `enum11_1.cj` and `enum11_2.cj`; for `enum11_3.cj` it reports exactly outer `AMBIGUOUS_ARG_TYPE` for `Time.Day(getTime<Int32>)` and `GENERIC_TYPE_SHOULD_BE_USED_WITH_TYPE_ARGUMENT` for bare `getTime`. `external/cangjie_compiler/src/Sema/TypeCheckCall.cpp:302-318,1489-1564,1596-1665`, `TypeCheckGeneric.cpp:214-237`, and `TypeCheckExpr/NameReferenceExpr.cpp:327-387` resolve each function candidate against the outer argument mapping, ignore bare generic functions, and distinguish different resulting function types from same-type declaration ambiguity.
- Kotlin counterpart files consulted: `external/kotlin/compiler/fir/resolve/src/org/jetbrains/kotlin/fir/resolve/calls/stages/ResolutionStages.kt` (`CheckCallableReferenceExpectedType`, `EagerResolveOfCallableReferences`), `FirCallResolver.kt`, `calls/candidate/CandidateCollector.kt`, `transformers/FirCallCompletionResultsWriterTransformer.kt`, and FIR resolved-error-reference utilities.
- CFIR owner files changed: `cfir/providers/src/org/cangnova/cangjie/cfir/resolve/providers/CfirCallableOwnerUseSiteSubstitution.kt`; `cfir/resolve/src/org/cangnova/cangjie/cfir/resolve/calls/ConeResolutionAtom.kt`; `calls/candidate/CallKind.kt`; `calls/stages/ArgumentCheckingProcessor.kt`; `calls/stages/CfirCheckCallableReferenceExpectedType.kt`; `body/CfirCallResolver.kt`; `inference/PostponedArgumentsAnalyzer.kt`; `inference/CfirCallCompletionResultsWriterTransformer.kt`; and `cfir/semantics/src/org/cangnova/cangjie/cfir/diagnostic/ResolutionDiagnostic.kt`.
- repair principle: callable-reference alternatives are candidate-local branches of the containing call's constraint system; their specific failure diagnostic owns applicability and final error attribution, while only the selected outer candidate commits expression state.
- fixtures covered: PSI and non-PSI `enum11_1.cj`, `enum11_2.cj`, and `enum11_3.cj`; guards were `enum13`, `enum15`, `enum16`, `enum16_1`, `enum28`, `enum32_1`, and `enum33`.
- fixture correction: `enum11_3.cj` now marks the complete `getTime` token for `GENERIC_TYPE_SHOULD_BE_USED_WITH_TYPE_ARGUMENT`, as required by the project Diagnostic Range Policy instead of cjc's single-character CLI anchor.
- verification commands: the six-test enum11 PSI/LT regression; the combined enum11 plus repaired-enum guard slices; then both complete PSI/LT `llt.enum.EnumGenerated` classes.
- verification outcome: enum11 passed 6/6 without debug traces; the seven guard fixtures passed 14/14; the complete Enum run executed 138 tests with 30 remaining failures, and no enum11 fixture remained in either failure list.

## Qualified enum owner selection and Iterable protocol element extraction

- problem type: an explicit enum qualifier must determine the constructor owner even under a different expected enum type, and `for-in` element types must come from the concrete type's instantiated `Iterable<E>` supertype.
- root cause: enum tower collection applied expected-owner filtering to both bare enum sugar and explicit `E.C`, so `RGBColor.Red` was rejected under expected `Option<RGBColor>`. After that was corrected, `inferIterableElementType` still inspected only arrays, Range, or the concrete receiver's own first type argument; a non-generic `RGBColor` implementing `Iterable<RGBColor>` therefore produced an error loop-variable type.
- official Cangjie evidence: cjc 1.0.5 accepts `enum32_1.cj`. `external/cangjie_compiler/src/Sema/TypeCheckExpr/LoopExprs.cpp:13-34,90-126` promotes the iterable expression to the unique `Iterable<E>` view and uses `E` as the loop-pattern type; `external/cangjie_runtime/stdlib/libs/std/core/iterator.cj:14-31` defines `Iterable<E>.iterator(): Iterator<E>` and `Iterator<T>.next(): Option<T>`.
- Kotlin counterpart files consulted: PSI `PsiRawFirBuilder.kt:3324-3407` and LightTree `LightTreeRawFirExpressionBuilder.kt:1290-1382`, which desugar for-loops to ordinary `iterator`/`hasNext`/`next` calls; `FirExpressionsResolveTransformer.kt:621-694` then resolves those calls through the normal call pipeline. CFIR's current dedicated for-in node uses the corresponding-supertype type-system seam until full convention-call desugaring is adopted.
- CFIR owner files changed: `cfir/resolve/src/org/cangnova/cangjie/cfir/resolve/calls/tower/TowerLevelHandler.kt`; `cfir/resolve/src/org/cangnova/cangjie/cfir/resolve/ResolveUtils.kt`; and `cfir/resolve/src/org/cangnova/cangjie/cfir/resolve/body/CfirExpressionsResolveTransformer.kt`.
- repair principle: expected enum owners refine only unqualified sugar, while protocol element types are read from the unique instantiated protocol supertype through the shared type checker rather than guessed from the concrete receiver shape.
- fixtures covered: PSI and non-PSI `enum32_1.cj`; the complete enum11 and repaired-enum slices guarded the owner-filtering change.
- fixture correction: none.
- verification commands: focused `enum32_1` with enum11; the remaining repaired-enum guard slice; then both complete PSI/LT `llt.enum.EnumGenerated` classes.
- verification outcome: the focused slice passed 8/8, the remaining guards passed 12/12, and the complete Enum run left 15 identical failing fixtures per entry path with `enum32_1.cj` absent.

## Mutable match subjects must not be narrowed from declaration initializers

- problem type: unreachable-pattern analysis for mutable enum subjects must use the subject's current flow facts, not the value assigned at declaration.
- root cause: `CfirMatchUnreachablePatternChecker` recursively followed local-variable initializers for both known enum constructors and known subject rows, permanently narrowing `var` subjects after later mutation or reassignment.
- official Cangjie evidence: `cjc 1.0.5` accepts `enum15.cj` and `enum32_1.cj` without diagnostics, and reports only the independent class-name-as-value plus pattern-mismatch errors in `enum16_1.cj`; `external/cangjie_compiler/src/Sema/PatternUsefulness.cpp:902-918,1019-1067` computes reachability from the selector's current pattern matrix, while flow facts are maintained separately by semantic analysis.
- Kotlin counterpart files consulted: `external/kotlin/compiler/fir/resolve/src/org/jetbrains/kotlin/fir/resolve/FirWhenExhaustivenessComputer.kt` and `FirControlFlowStatementsResolveTransformer.kt`; exhaustiveness consumes subject/branch facts and does not backtrack declaration initializers in the checker.
- CFIR owner files changed: `cfir/checkers/src/org/cangnova/cangjie/cfir/analysis/checkers/expression/CfirMatchUnreachablePatternChecker.kt`.
- repair principle: only the subject expression's own resolved constructor/literal/tuple shape may seed static usefulness; local-variable value domains must come from CFG/DFA rather than initializer syntax.
- fixtures covered: PSI and non-PSI `enum15.cj`, `enum16_1.cj`, and `enum32_1.cj`.
- verification commands: `./gradlew.bat :cfir:analysis-tests:test --tests '*EnumGenerated.testEnum15' --tests '*EnumGenerated.testEnum161' --tests '*EnumGenerated.testEnum32' --no-configuration-cache --no-daemon --max-workers=1 --console=plain`.
- verification outcome: the combined repaired-enum regression was `BUILD SUCCESSFUL`; all six PSI/LT tests passed after the independent initializer-scope repair restored the two official diagnostics in `enum16_1.cj`.

## Enum pattern constructor identity includes payload arity

- problem type: enum patterns with same-named zero- and one-argument constructors must select the declaration matching the pattern payload arity.
- root cause: `CfirMatchPatternLegalityChecker` selected the first constructor by name alone, so `case e(d)` could inspect `D.d(Int64)` instead of zero-argument `D.d` and report an arity error rather than the real non-exhaustiveness.
- official Cangjie evidence: `cjc 1.0.5` reports only non-exhaustive patterns for `enum28.cj`, with witness `e(d(_))`; `external/cangjie_compiler/src/Sema/TypeCheckPattern.cpp:490-570` requires payload count to equal constructor parameter count, and `PatternUsefulness.cpp:227-237,583-600,646-661,902-918` preserves constructor arity in usefulness witnesses.
- Kotlin counterpart files consulted: Kotlin FIR pattern/when exhaustiveness architecture was used only as framework guidance; constructor identity remains Cangjie-specific and is represented in CFIR's shared match model by name plus arity.
- CFIR owner files changed: `cfir/checkers/src/org/cangnova/cangjie/cfir/analysis/checkers/expression/CfirMatchPatternLegalityChecker.kt`; `cfir/checkers/src/org/cangnova/cangjie/cfir/analysis/checkers/expression/CfirMatchExhaustivenessChecker.kt` restores the match-expression diagnostic range on both PSI and LightTree paths.
- repair principle: resolve enum pattern constructors against the expected enum owner and exact payload arity, preserving a distinct arity-mismatch result while letting valid patterns reach shared exhaustiveness analysis.
- fixtures covered: PSI and non-PSI `enum28.cj`.
- verification commands: `./gradlew.bat :cfir:analysis-tests:test --tests '*EnumGenerated.testEnum28' --no-configuration-cache --no-daemon --max-workers=1 --console=plain`.
- verification outcome: `BUILD SUCCESSFUL`; both PSI/LT tests passed.

## Local declaration initializer lookup excludes the declaration being introduced

- problem type: a local variable or pattern binding is not visible inside its own initializer; lookup must continue to outer values, classifiers, or enum-constructor sugar before the new binding enters scope.
- root cause: `CfirDeclarationsResolveTransformer` stored ordinary variables and pattern bindings before resolving their initializer, and `CfirExpressionsResolveTransformer` then converted a successful self-symbol candidate directly into `ConeUnresolvedNameError`. This stopped outer-scope/classifier lookup and enum-sugar fallback.
- official Cangjie evidence: `external/cangjie_compiler/src/Sema/LookUpImpl.cpp:449-461,530-555,564-570` excludes the current `VarDecl` during its initializer and continues through outer scopes. With a top-level `class A2`, `var A2 = A2` resolves the RHS to the class and `TypeCheckReference.cpp:624-631` reports `sema_ref_not_be_type`; without the class, `NameReferenceExpr.cpp:426-441` and `EnumSugarTargetsFinder.cpp:24-63` continue to the zero-payload enum constructor.
- Kotlin counterpart files consulted: `external/kotlin/compiler/fir/resolve/src/org/jetbrains/kotlin/fir/resolve/transformers/FirDeclarationsResolveTransformer.kt:657-703`, which transforms the initializer and stores its return type before adding the variable to the local context.
- CFIR owner files changed: `cfir/resolve/src/org/cangnova/cangjie/cfir/resolve/body/CfirDeclarationsResolveTransformer.kt` and `cfir/resolve/src/org/cangnova/cangjie/cfir/resolve/body/CfirExpressionsResolveTransformer.kt`.
- repair principle: declaration scope begins after initializer resolution; field-initializer self-reference remains a separate member-initialization rule, while local lookup naturally falls through to outer declarations or unresolved-name recovery.
- fixtures covered: PSI and non-PSI `enum16.cj` and `enum16_1.cj`; the `var.cj` self-reference line remained `UNRESOLVED_REFERENCE` as a guard, while that fixture still has an unrelated lambda type-mismatch expectation difference.
- verification commands: focused `*EnumGenerated.testEnum16`, `*EnumGenerated.testEnum161`, and `*VarGenerated.testVar`; then the combined repaired-enum regression selecting `enum13`, `enum15`, `enum16`, `enum16_1`, `enum28`, `enum32_1`, and `enum33`.
- verification outcome: both `enum16` and `enum16_1` passed on PSI and LightTree; the combined 14-test enum regression was `BUILD SUCCESSFUL in 1m 45s`. Fresh `var.cj` output retained the expected unresolved self-reference.

## Bare parameterized enum-constructor access legality

- problem type: a parameterized enum constructor referenced without call or pattern syntax must resolve to the lexical/extended enum owner and report only `ENUM_CONSTRUCTOR_WITH_PARAM_MUST_HAVE_ARGS`.
- root cause: lexical enum-owner filtering did not consistently cover extended enum types, argument mapping treated a bare constructor reference as a zero-argument call and added `NO_VALUE_FOR_PARAMETER`, and the function-reference legality checker did not recognize `CfirEnumConstructorSymbol` as a callable symbol.
- official Cangjie evidence: cjc 1.0.5 reports exactly two `sema_enum_constructor_with_param_must_have_args` diagnostics in `enum33.cj`, on the bare `e` in `E2.foo` and `extend E2.goo`. `external/cangjie_compiler/src/Sema/TypeCheckReference.cpp:310-364`, `TypeCheckExpr/NameReferenceExpr.cpp:426-454`, and `Diags.cpp:43-64,291-311` show that the dedicated bare-constructor diagnostic terminates reference checking before call argument diagnostics.
- Kotlin counterpart files consulted: callable lookup/filtering and callable-reference legality paths in Kotlin FIR tower resolution; Kotlin supplied framework placement only because enum-constructor sugar is Cangjie-specific.
- CFIR owner files changed: `cfir/resolve/src/org/cangnova/cangjie/cfir/resolve/calls/tower/TowerLevelHandler.kt`, `cfir/resolve/src/org/cangnova/cangjie/cfir/resolve/calls/stages/CfirMapArguments.kt`, and `cfir/checkers/src/org/cangnova/cangjie/cfir/analysis/checkers/expression/CfirFunctionReferenceLegalityChecker.kt`.
- repair principle: bind bare enum sugar to the semantic enum owner before legality checking, and keep bare-reference legality separate from real call argument mapping.
- fixtures covered: PSI and non-PSI `enum33.cj`; `enum13.cj` was used as an enum-resolution guard.
- verification commands: `\.\gradlew.bat :cfir:analysis-tests:test --tests '*EnumGenerated.testEnum13' --tests '*EnumGenerated.testEnum33' --no-configuration-cache --no-daemon --max-workers=1 --console=plain`.
- verification outcome: `BUILD SUCCESSFUL in 1m 38s`; all four PSI/LT tests passed.

## Target-typed enum payload calls and reentrant tower collection

- problem type: an unqualified enum payload call used as a constructor argument must be reselected from its outer expected enum type, while nested call resolution must not overwrite the outer call's candidate state or re-inject an already completed constraint system.
- root cause: `CfirTowerResolver` reused one mutable collector/manager across reentrant resolutions, so resolving inner `Some(Sub())` cleared the outer `Other(...)` candidates. After isolating that state, target-typed re-resolution still selected imported `Option.Some` because enum tower collection ignored an expected enum owner; fully completed nested candidates were then reused or added as subsystems, causing stale `A<Sub>` mismatches and duplicate type-variable fixation.
- official Cangjie evidence: cjc 1.0.5 compiles and executes `enum13.cj` successfully, reporting only the unused `ret2` warning. `external/cangjie_compiler/src/Sema/TypeCheckCall.cpp:1165-1178,2328-2337`, `TypeChecker.cpp:1065-1075,1403-1415`, and `EnumSugarTargetsFinder.cpp:24-117` show that `Other.init(a: A<Base>)` supplies `A<Base>` to `Some(Sub())`, refines the unqualified constructor to `A.Some`, and caches checks by target type. `NameReferenceExpr.cpp:580-587,702-715,924-969` keeps `Other.value` typed as `A<Base>`.
- Kotlin counterpart files consulted: `external/kotlin/compiler/fir/resolve/src/org/jetbrains/kotlin/fir/resolve/calls/tower/TowerLevelHandler.kt`, `calls/candidate/CandidateCollector.kt`, and `FirCallResolver.kt` for per-resolution candidate ownership and call-kind separation.
- CFIR owner files changed: `cfir/resolve/src/org/cangnova/cangjie/cfir/resolve/body/CfirTowerResolver.kt`, `cfir/resolve/src/org/cangnova/cangjie/cfir/resolve/calls/tower/TowerLevelHandler.kt`, `cfir/resolve/src/org/cangnova/cangjie/cfir/resolve/calls/stages/ArgumentCheckingProcessor.kt`, `cfir/resolve/src/org/cangnova/cangjie/cfir/resolve/inference/CfirCallCompleter.kt`, and `cfir/resolve/src/org/cangnova/cangjie/cfir/resolve/calls/candidate/Candidate.kt`.
- repair principle: candidate collection is invocation-owned state; expected enum owners participate before tower stopping, and only unfinished nested candidates propagate constraint subsystems while fully completed calls expose their final expression type.
- fixtures covered: PSI and non-PSI `enum13.cj`; guards included `enum33.cj`, `enum_interface_box_01.cj`, and `enum_interface_box_02.cj`.
- verification commands: focused non-PSI `testEnum13`; combined `*EnumGenerated.testEnum13` and `*EnumGenerated.testEnum33`; then a guard run including both enum interface-box tests.
- verification outcome: focused non-PSI `enum13` was `BUILD SUCCESSFUL in 7m 11s`; the combined PSI/LT `enum13` and `enum33` regression was `BUILD SUCCESSFUL in 1m 38s`; both interface-box guards passed after expected-owner filtering was restricted to actual enum classifiers.

## Inherited static/non-static member-name conflict classification

- problem type: an inherited member whose static status differs from the same-name parent member must report the dedicated static/non-static conflict before declaration-kind inconsistency, across classes, interfaces, functions, properties, and `redef` declarations.
- root cause: 16 class fixtures still expected `INHERIT_MEMBER_KIND_INCONSISTENT` even though CFIR already followed the official priority rule and emitted `STATIC_AND_NON_STATIC_MEMBER_CANNOT_HAVE_SAME_NAME` on the complete child member name.
- official Cangjie evidence: cjc 1.0.5 probes for class/interface, function/property, mixed declaration kinds, and `redef` all emitted `sema_static_and_non_static_member_cannot_have_same_name`; `class_shadow8` confirmed that differing static status takes priority even when the AST declaration kinds also differ. `external/cangjie_compiler/src/Sema/InheritanceChecker/StructInheritanceChecker.cpp:714-734,1082-1104` checks static status first and returns after that diagnostic; only equal static status with different declaration kinds emits `sema_inherit_member_kind_inconsistent`. Definitions are in `DiagnosticSema.def:222-223` and `DiagRefactor/DiagnosticSema.def:101-103`.
- Kotlin counterpart files consulted: `external/kotlin/compiler/fir/checkers/src/org/jetbrains/kotlin/fir/analysis/checkers/declaration/FirOverrideChecker.kt`, `FirConflictsDeclarationChecker.kt`, and `FirImplementationMismatchChecker.kt` for separating inherited override relations from same-container declaration conflicts.
- CFIR owner files changed: fixture corrections only; the shared emitting owner remains `CfirInheritanceDeepChecker.checkInheritedMemberKindConsistency`.
- repair principle: preserve the official static-status-first diagnostic classification and correct stale expectations, while retaining the project full-token range over the conflicting member name instead of cjc's declaration-start single-character anchor.
- fixtures covered: PSI and non-PSI `class_no_override_modifier_invalid_2/3/4.cj`, `interface_no_override_modifier_invalid_2/3/4.cj`, `class_shadow1/2/3/4/6/7/8.cj`, and `class_redef_2/4/6.cj`.
- fixture correction: replaced `INHERIT_MEMBER_KIND_INCONSISTENT` with `STATIC_AND_NON_STATIC_MEMBER_CANNOT_HAVE_SAME_NAME` in all 16 fixtures, preserving companion diagnostics such as `CANNOT_OVERRIDE` and `REDEF_INSTANCE_ERROR` and preserving complete identifier ranges.
- verification commands: the four-pattern class override/shadow/redef regression, followed by both complete PSI/LT `ClassGenerated` suites.
- verification outcome: the pattern regression selected 96 tests and left only the unrelated default-implementation conflicts plus `class_redef_11/12`; after the next shared-scope repair, complete `ClassGenerated` XML reported 232 tests / 11 failures for each entry path, with none of these 16 fixtures remaining.

## Distinct interface default implementations in inherited-member merging

- problem type: when different interfaces provide the same instantiated default-member signature, the derived class must retain both implementation origins and require an explicit implementation; duplicate paths to the same interface default must still merge.
- root cause: `CfirClassUseSiteMemberScope.mergeEquivalentInheritedFunctions()` keyed inherited functions only by static status plus pre- and post-substitution signatures. Distinct defaults such as `A.foo` and `B.foo` therefore collapsed to one symbol before `CfirNotImplementedOverrideChecker` could observe multiple interface owners.
- official Cangjie evidence: cjc 1.0.5 reports `sema_interface_member_must_be_implemented` on the derived class in `class_no_override_modifier_invalid_5/6/8.cj`. `external/cangjie_compiler/src/Sema/InheritanceChecker/MemberSignature.h:25`, `MergeInheritedMemberHelper.cpp:185-200`, and `StructInheritanceChecker.cpp:977-982` preserve multiple default origins through `shouldBeImplemented`; `DiagnosticSema.def:217` defines the diagnostic.
- Kotlin counterpart files consulted: `external/kotlin/compiler/fir/resolve/src/org/jetbrains/kotlin/fir/scopes/impl/AbstractFirUseSiteMemberScope.kt`, `FirTypeIntersectionScopeContext.kt`, and `external/kotlin/compiler/fir/checkers/src/org/jetbrains/kotlin/fir/analysis/checkers/declaration/FirNotImplementedOverrideChecker.kt` with `FirHelpers.getImplementationStatus()` / `AMBIGUOUSLY_INHERITED`.
- CFIR owner files changed: `cfir/providers/src/org/cangnova/cangjie/cfir/scopes/impl/CfirClassUseSiteMemberScope.kt`.
- repair principle: preserve default implementation provenance in the shared inherited-function merge key, using the declaration owner only for default members so same-interface diamond paths still deduplicate while different interface defaults remain available to every downstream scope consumer.
- fixtures covered: PSI and non-PSI `class_no_override_modifier_invalid_5.cj`, `_6.cj`, and `_8.cj`; `_7.cj` is the substitution/provenance guard and remained green.
- fixture correction: none.
- verification commands: `.\gradlew.bat :cfir:analysis-tests:test --tests '*ClassGenerated.testClassNoOverrideModifierInvalid*' --no-daemon --max-workers=1 --console=plain`; then both complete PSI/LT `ClassGenerated` suites.
- verification outcome: the focused family was `BUILD SUCCESSFUL`; complete `ClassGenerated` XML reported 232 tests / 11 failures on each entry path, with `_5`, `_6`, `_7`, and `_8` all absent from the failure lists.

## Explicit callable type arguments and value-argument mismatch classification

- problem type: after explicit callable type arguments instantiate a function signature, value arguments must be checked against the substituted parameter types and reported as ordinary `TYPE_MISMATCH`, independently from type-argument arity and resolution errors.
- root cause: the central constraint-mismatch mapper did not distinguish callable-owned explicit type arguments from generic types appearing only on the receiver, so value-argument incompatibilities could use the wrong argument diagnostic category; stale fixtures also treated a type-position undeclared name as an expression unresolved reference.
- official Cangjie evidence: `external/cangjie_compiler/src/Sema/TypeCheckGeneric.cpp` `CheckCallGenericDeclInstantiation`, `CheckGenericDeclInstantiation`, and `GetGenericType` report generic-argument no-match only when the declaration/type-argument counts differ, then instantiate the callable through `GenerateTypeMapping` before checking values; `external/cangjie_compiler/src/Sema/Diags.cpp:202,220` emits `sema_mismatched_types` for the resulting value mismatch. An independent cjc 1.0.5 probe for `data.getFoo<Roo<Int32>>(roo)` reported `sema_mismatched_types` on the complete `roo` range with expected `Roo<Roo<Int32>>` and actual `Roo<Int32>`.
- Kotlin counterpart files consulted: `external/kotlin/compiler/fir/resolve/src/org/jetbrains/kotlin/fir/resolve/calls/stages/TypeArgumentMapping.kt`, `CreateFreshTypeVariableSubstitutorStage.kt`, `CheckArguments.kt`, `ArgumentCheckingProcessor.kt`, and `external/kotlin/compiler/fir/semantics/src/org/jetbrains/kotlin/fir/resolve/inference/model/FirConstraintPositionAndErrors.kt`.
- CFIR owner files changed: `cfir/checkers/src/org/cangnova/cangjie/cfir/analysis/diagnostics/coneDiagnosticToCfirDiagnostic.kt`, plus fixture corrections in `function1.cj` and `class3.cj`.
- repair principle: identify explicit type arguments only when they belong to the selected callable, then let the shared substituted-parameter argument checker and central diagnostic mapper classify the value mismatch; receiver type arguments never masquerade as callable type arguments.
- fixtures covered: PSI and non-PSI `function1.cj` and `class3.cj`; `class5_test.cj` remained the constructor analogue guard.
- fixture correction: `function1.cj` now expects `TYPE_MISMATCH` for `Add<Float64>(a)` and `UNDECLARED_TYPE_NAME` for type-position `T`; `class3.cj` includes the official `RETURN_TYPE_MISMATCH`, `NO_VALUE_FOR_PARAMETER`, and both `TYPE_MISMATCH` expectations, including the explicit-callable-type-argument guard on `roo`.
- verification commands: the four-method critical Generics regression selecting `testFunction1`, `testClass3`, `testFunction14Test`, and `testGenericParameters2`; then both complete PSI/LT `GenericsGenerated` suites.
- verification outcome: the critical regression passed 8/8; the complete regression passed 296/296.

## Enum constructor value-domain flow and nested target typing

- problem type: local enum values constructed before a `match` must carry their exact constructor tag through assignments and control-flow joins, while nested same-name enum constructor calls must be resolved independently inside each outer candidate's expected-type branch.
- root cause: CFIR had no function-level enum constructor value-domain, so `UNREACHABLE_PATTERN` could not use constructor facts from local declarations; nested enum calls were also committed without candidate-local target typing. The initial repair exposed two framework defects: fixed fresh variables were still treated as constraint targets during same-classifier decomposition, and CHIR-derived value facts were consumed even when a Sema `NON_EXHAUSTIVE_MATCH` error would have stopped the official pipeline.
- official Cangjie evidence: cjc 1.0.5 reports `chir_unreachable_pattern` for `enum12.cj`, `enum14.cj`, and `enum25_3.cj`. `TranslateCallExpr.cpp:635-675`, `ValueAnalysis.h:1334,1373,1386-1445`, `ConstAnalysis.cpp:543`, `UnreachableBranchCheck.cpp:57-162`, and `Engine.h:195-224` show enum constructor IDs flowing through CHIR value analysis. `EnumSugarTargetsFinder.cpp:24-83`, `TypeCheckCall.cpp:1165-1179,1365-1405,1957-2005,2014,2096-2143,2750-2774`, and `TypeChecker.cpp:509-523,1065-1075,1228` show nested arguments checked under each candidate's parameter type and committed only after candidate selection. For `enum28.cj`, cjc reports only `sema_nonexhuastive_patterns`; `CompileStrategy.cpp:572-588` proves that this Sema error prevents CHIR unreachable analysis. Enum constructor identity is declaration/ordinal based in `TranslateCallExpr.cpp:650-657` and `TranslateMatchExpr.cpp:251`, so same-name constructors remain distinct by payload arity.
