- Kotlin counterpart files consulted: `FirDataFlowAnalyzer.kt:1097-1108`, `FirLocalVariableAssignmentAnalyzer.kt:151-203,268-294`, FIR DFA symbol-value modeling, `FirWhenExhaustivenessComputer.kt`, `NewConstraintSystemImpl.fixVariable`, `ConstraintInjector.kt`, and `InferenceUtils.buildCurrentSubstitutor`.
- CFIR owner files changed: `CfirMatchSubjectValueDomainData.kt`, `CfirEnumTagFlowAnalyzer.kt`, `CfirDataFlowAnalyzer.kt`, `CfirLocalVariableAssignmentAnalyzer.kt`, `CfirMatchUnreachablePatternChecker.kt`, `ArgumentCheckingProcessor.kt`, `CfirCallCompleter.kt`, and `Candidate.kt`.
- repair principle: preserve exact constructor facts and callable write effects in a function-level DFA carrier, resolve nested enum calls in isolated outer-candidate contexts, and only inject constraints into variables still present in `notFixedTypeVariables` after applying the current fixed-variable substitutor.
- fixtures covered: PSI and non-PSI `enum12.cj`, `enum14.cj`, and `enum25_3.cj`; regression guards included `enum13.cj`, `enum15.cj`, `enum16_1.cj`, `enum28.cj`, and `enum32_1.cj`.
- fixture correction: none.
- verification commands: the combined PSI/LT focused regression selecting `testEnum12`, `testEnum13`, `testEnum14`, `testEnum15`, `testEnum161`, `testEnum253`, `testEnum28`, and `testEnum321`; then both complete PSI/LT `llt.enum.EnumGenerated` classes.
- verification outcome: the focused regression passed 16/16. The complete Enum regression ran 138 tests with 24 failures across 12 PSI/LT-symmetric fixtures; `enum12`, `enum13`, `enum14`, `enum25_3`, and `enum28` were absent from the failure list, with no new regression.

## Candidate-bearing nested enum calls and ambiguity dominance

- problem type: nested same-named enum constructor calls must remain candidate-local until the outer call selects a branch; when every successful outer ambiguity branch independently explains the same nested call, the outer ambiguity structurally dominates the inner ambiguity diagnostic without choosing an inner winner.
- root cause: speculative nested resolution completed and mutated shared call trees too early, literal types were reused after candidate-specific concretization, enum constructors exposed declaration return types instead of substituted owner enum types, and the first ambiguity-dominance implementation permanently replaced the original nested call diagnostic/type with an unreported wrapper.
- official Cangjie evidence: cjc 1.0.5 accepts `Out(Day(Year(3)))`; for `Day(Year(1))` across two enum owners it reports only the outer `sema_multiple_constructor_in_enum`. `external/cangjie_compiler/src/Sema/TypeCheckCall.cpp:1165-1179,1957-2005,2096-2143,2583-2606` checks nested calls under each outer candidate target and classifies the final enum-constructor ambiguity without committing an arbitrary branch.
- Kotlin counterpart files consulted: Kotlin FIR `ConeResolutionAtoms.kt`, `CandidateFactory.kt`, `CheckArguments.kt`, `FirCallResolver.kt`, and `FirCallCompletionResultsWriterTransformer.kt` for candidate-bearing nested atoms, subsystem merging, and winner-only recursive completion.
- CFIR owner files changed: `cfir/resolve/src/org/cangnova/cangjie/cfir/resolve/calls/stages/ArgumentCheckingProcessor.kt`, `cfir/resolve/src/org/cangnova/cangjie/cfir/resolve/calls/candidate/Candidate.kt`, `cfir/resolve/src/org/cangnova/cangjie/cfir/resolve/inference/CfirCallCompletionResultsWriterTransformer.kt`, `cfir/resolve/src/org/cangnova/cangjie/cfir/resolve/ResolveUtils.kt`, `cfir/resolve/src/org/cangnova/cangjie/cfir/resolve/body/CfirCallResolver.kt`, `cfir/semantics/src/org/cangnova/cangjie/cfir/diagnostic/ConeDiagnostic.kt`, and `cfir/checkers/src/org/cangnova/cangjie/cfir/analysis/collectors/components/ErrorNodeDiagnosticCollectorComponent.kt`.
- repair principle: speculative resolution owns detached candidate state and immutable dominance metadata; only the selected outer candidate writes replacements, while diagnostic collection suppresses an inner ambiguity solely when the containing outer ambiguity carrier references that exact diagnostic object.
- fixtures covered: PSI and non-PSI `enum14.cj` and `enum14_1.cj`; guards included `enum10.cj`, `enum13.cj`, `enum28.cj`, and the complete Enum generated slice.
- verification commands: focused `*EnumGenerated.testEnum14` plus `*EnumGenerated.testEnum141`; then `:cfir:analysis-tests:test --tests '*llt.enum.EnumGenerated*' -x :cfir:analysis-tests:generateTestGeneratorForCfirAnalysisTestsTests --no-configuration-cache --no-daemon --max-workers=1 --console=plain`.
- verification outcome: focused PSI/LT `enum14` and `enum14_1` passed 4/4; the complete Enum slice ran 142 tests with 20 failures across 10 unrelated PSI/LT-symmetric fixtures, and both repaired fixtures were absent from the failure list.

## Target-typed Rune literal conversion diagnostics

- problem type: a Rune literal that cannot convert to its contextual target type must report the dedicated `CANNOT_CONVERT_LITERAL` diagnostic in calls, declarations, default values, ranges, and other shared target-typing consumers.
- root cause: Rune literals were typed as `Rune`, then every incompatible target was flattened into the caller-specific `ARGUMENT_TYPE_MISMATCH` or `TYPE_MISMATCH`; the shared mismatch classifier had no access to the original literal expression and the project diagnostic surface lacked `CANNOT_CONVERT_LITERAL`.
- official Cangjie evidence: cjc 1.0.5 reports `sema_cannot_convert_literal` with `cannot convert a character literal to type 'Int32'` for both `TimeUnit.Month(r'a')` and an ordinary function parameter. `external/cangjie_compiler/src/Sema/TypeCheckExpr/LitConstExpr.cpp:113-127,193-209` routes Rune target checking through `ChkLitConstExprOfTypeChar`, and `TypeChecker.cpp:1122-1135` applies target-typed literal checking independently of call kind.
- Kotlin counterpart files consulted: Kotlin FIR `ArgumentCheckingProcessor.kt`, `coneDiagnosticToFirDiagnostic.kt`, and `IntegerLiteralAndOperatorApproximationTransformer.kt` for separating literal typing from call-argument diagnostic mapping; the Cangjie-specific literal-conversion classification comes only from official Cangjie evidence.
- CFIR owner files changed: `cfir/checkers/checkers-component-generator/src/org/cangnova/cangjie/cfir/checkers/generator/diagnostics/CfirDiagnosticsList.kt`, `cfir/checkers/src/org/cangnova/cangjie/cfir/analysis/diagnostics/CfirErrorsDefaultMessages.kt`, `CfirTypeSemanticsDiagnostics.kt`, `coneDiagnosticToCfirDiagnostic.kt`, and the shared initializer/range/try mismatch callers; fixture corrections in `enum10.cj`, `depth_call.cj`, and `var_literal_type_incompatible.cj`.
- repair principle: preserve the original literal expression at the shared mismatch-classification seam and emit a parameterized literal-conversion diagnostic before context-specific generic mismatch diagnostics.
- fixtures covered: PSI and non-PSI `enum10.cj`, `depth_call.cj`, and `var_literal_type_incompatible.cj`.
- verification commands: focused LT `EnumGenerated.testEnum10`; focused PSI `EnumGenerated.testEnum10` with both `testEnum141` guards; `*FunctionGenerated.testDepthCall`; and `*VarGenerated.testVarLiteralTypeIncompatible`.
- verification outcome: `enum10`, `depth_call`, and `var_literal_type_incompatible` all passed on PSI and LightTree; the complete Enum slice also omitted `enum10` from its 20 remaining failures.

## Arity-only failed-candidate selection and qualified-call argument ranges

- problem type: argument-count diagnostics must select the overload whose accepted arity best explains the call, mark a concrete extra argument when one exists, and mark only the selector call's parenthesized argument list for missing parameters on qualified calls.
- root cause: ordinary calls did not reuse the arity-distance reduction already present for delegating constructors, so a one-parameter enum candidate could become the representative error for a three-argument call even when a two-parameter candidate was closer. Separately, PSI and LightTree `VALUE_ARGUMENTS`/`VALUE_ARGUMENTS_LIST` positioning recognized only root call nodes and fell back to the entire qualified expression.
- official Cangjie evidence: range-only repair; the project Diagnostic Range Policy is authoritative. Across the repository, concrete extra arguments are marked individually, while `NO_VALUE_FOR_PARAMETER` normally covers the callee selector's complete parenthesized argument list.
- Kotlin counterpart files consulted: Kotlin FIR `FirArgumentsToParametersMapper.kt:208-211,249-266,302-313`, `coneDiagnosticToFirDiagnostic.kt:213-226`, and `FirDiagnosticsList.kt:756-761`; Kotlin stores the actual extra argument and uses a value-arguments positioning strategy for missing parameters.
- CFIR owner files changed: `cfir/resolve/src/org/cangnova/cangjie/cfir/resolve/body/CfirCallResolver.kt`, `common/diagnostics/src/org/cangnova/cangjie/cfir/diagnostics/PositioningStrategies.kt`, and `LightTreePositioningStrategies.kt`; fixture range correction in `cfir/analysis-tests/testData/llt/enum/enum1.cj`.
- repair principle: choose the most explanatory pure-arity failure before normal most-specific reduction, then let shared diagnostics positioning derive ranges from the actual extra argument or the selector-side argument list instead of reconstructing ranges in the converter.
- fixtures covered: PSI and non-PSI `enum16_2.cj`, `enum1_optionalBITOR.cj`, and `enum1.cj`; repository search found 26 `TOO_MANY_ARGUMENTS` markers and 30 `NO_VALUE_FOR_PARAMETER` markers as the wider guard family.
- verification commands: focused `*EnumGenerated.testEnum162` plus `*EnumGenerated.testEnum1OptionalBITOR`; complete `*llt.enum.EnumGenerated*` rerun; focused guard rerun selecting `testEnum1`, `testEnum162`, and `testEnum1OptionalBITOR`.
- verification outcome: the two triggering fixtures passed on PSI and LightTree; the complete Enum slice dropped from 20 to 18 failures and neither trigger remained. The only newly exposed difference was the stale whole-call range in `enum1.cj`; after correcting it to the shared argument-list policy, the six-test PSI/LT guard passed.

## Bare generic enum/static calls and ideal-literal inference

- problem type: a static member declared on a generic enum or its `extend` must infer the bare owner type arguments from the selected extension target, while ideal numeric literals inside a partially inferred generic call must contribute their concrete default primitive type soon enough for builtin operator resolution.
- root cause: fresh-variable creation treated every bare generic owner uniformly, losing the concrete `extend E<Rune>` relay from the enum owner parameter to the extension parameter; ideal-literal constraints were also recorded as unresolved `LOWER(IdealInt)` bounds, so a nested generic call operand had no stable primitive type when the builtin `+` fast path ran.
- official Cangjie evidence: cjc 1.0.5 accepts `enum36.cj` without diagnostics and accepts `enum45_generic_extend_staticFunc.cj` with only the unused-variable warning. `external/cangjie_compiler/src/Sema/TypeCheckCall.cpp:192+` collects generic parameters for static members reached through a bare generic owner; `MultiTypeSubstUtils.cpp:360+` uses `GenerateTypeMapping` and `RelayMappingFromExtendToExtended` to connect extension parameters to the extended type; `LocalTypeArgumentSynthesis.cpp:558-590,1058+` adds ideal-literal constraints and applies `ResetIdealTypesInSubst` before final use.
- Kotlin counterpart files consulted: Kotlin FIR call-resolution fresh-variable creation, argument constraint generation, constraint-system completion, and builtin operator resolution paths corresponding to `CreateFreshTypeVariableSubstitutorStage.kt`, `ArgumentCheckingProcessor.kt`, and expression resolution.
- CFIR owner files changed: `cfir/resolve/src/org/cangnova/cangjie/cfir/resolve/calls/stages/CfirCreateFreshTypeVariableSubstitutorStage.kt`, `cfir/resolve/src/org/cangnova/cangjie/cfir/resolve/calls/stages/ArgumentCheckingProcessor.kt`, and `cfir/resolve/src/org/cangnova/cangjie/cfir/resolve/body/CfirExpressionsResolveTransformer.kt`.
- repair principle: construct the owner/extension substitution graph at candidate creation, normalize ideal literals to evidence-backed primitive bounds at the shared argument-constraint seam, and expose only a unique primitive bound to builtin operator lookup without fixing variables or mutating the nested call.
- fixtures covered: PSI and non-PSI `enum36.cj` and `enum45_generic_extend_staticFunc.cj`.
- fixture correction: none.
- verification commands: `.\\gradlew.bat :cfir:analysis-tests:test --rerun --tests '*EnumGenerated.testEnum36' --tests '*EnumGenerated.testEnum45GenericExtendStaticFunc' -x :cfir:analysis-tests:generateTestGeneratorForCfirAnalysisTestsTests --no-configuration-cache --no-daemon --max-workers=1 --console=plain`.
- verification outcome: `BUILD SUCCESSFUL in 7m 32s`; all four PSI/LightTree tests passed.

## Property accessor normal completion and immutable-assignment source ownership

- problem type: an accessor body that can complete normally without a value must be checked against its declared getter return type, and assignment to an immutable qualified property must report on the complete lvalue rather than only the terminal selector.
- root cause: function-body mismatch checking treated an empty block or statement-ending block as having no result instead of the normal-completion result `Unit`; assignment legality also selected the resolved access token source instead of the qualified lvalue source.
- official Cangjie evidence: cjc 1.0.5 reports `sema_type_mismatch` for `get() {}` together with `sema_immutable_property_with_setter`, and reports `sema_cannot_assign_to_immutable` on both complete property assignments in `enum17_prop2.cj`. The behavior is implemented by `external/cangjie_compiler/src/Sema/DeclAttributeChecker.cpp:375-401`, `TypeChecker.cpp:85-166,304-328`, `TypeCheckDecl.cpp:355-382`, `LegalityOfUsage/InitializationChecker.cpp:165-171,675-747`, and `Diagnostic/Diags.cpp:359-370`.
- Kotlin counterpart files consulted: `FirFunctionReturnTypeMismatchChecker.kt`, `FirReassignmentAndInvisibleSetterChecker.kt`, and the PSI/LightTree raw FIR accessor builders that propagate accessor return types.
- CFIR owner files changed: `cfir/checkers/src/org/cangnova/cangjie/cfir/analysis/checkers/expression/CfirFunctionBodyTypeMismatchChecker.kt` and `CfirAssignmentLegalityChecker.kt`.
- repair principle: model normal block completion as `Unit` at the shared function-body checker and derive immutable-assignment ranges from the lvalue expression owned by the assignment checker.
- fixtures covered: PSI and non-PSI `enum17_prop2.cj`.
- fixture correction: none.
- verification commands: `.\gradlew.bat :cfir:analysis-tests:test --rerun --tests '*EnumGenerated.testEnum17Prop2' --tests '*EnumGenerated.testEnum301' -x :cfir:analysis-tests:generateTestGeneratorForCfirAnalysisTestsTests --no-configuration-cache --no-daemon --max-workers=1 --console=plain`.
- verification outcome: `BUILD SUCCESSFUL in 2m 8s`; all four PSI/LightTree tests passed, including both `enum17_prop2` variants.

## Enum values are immutable assignment targets

- problem type: variables and enum constructor values are immutable assignment targets even when the enum constructor is referenced through enum sugar.
- root cause: assignment-target classification did not map `CfirEnumConstructorSymbol` to an immutable value, so a resolved enum value could bypass the common reassignment diagnostic.
- official Cangjie evidence: cjc 1.0.5 reports `sema_cannot_assign_to_immutable` for both `v = B` and `A = B` in a minimized enum probe; enum constructors without payload are values, not mutable storage locations.
- Kotlin counterpart files consulted: `FirReassignmentAndInvisibleSetterChecker.kt`, where enum entries participate in the same val-reassignment classification as other immutable values.
- CFIR owner files changed: `cfir/checkers/src/org/cangnova/cangjie/cfir/analysis/checkers/expression/CfirAssignmentLegalityChecker.kt`.
- repair principle: classify enum constructor symbols in the shared mutation-target model instead of adding an enum-only diagnostic branch at an assignment site.
- fixtures covered: PSI and non-PSI `enum30_1.cj`.
- fixture correction: none.
- verification commands: `.\gradlew.bat :cfir:analysis-tests:test --rerun --tests '*EnumGenerated.testEnum17Prop2' --tests '*EnumGenerated.testEnum301' -x :cfir:analysis-tests:generateTestGeneratorForCfirAnalysisTestsTests --no-configuration-cache --no-daemon --max-workers=1 --console=plain`.
- verification outcome: `BUILD SUCCESSFUL in 2m 8s`; all four PSI/LightTree tests passed, including both `enum30_1` variants.

## Enum instance constructor access and implicit invoke ownership

- problem type: `value.Entry` is an invalid enum instance member access, while `value.Entry()` for a zero-payload entry first denotes the enum value and then fails at the shared implicit-`invoke` owner.
- root cause: the ordinary failed call was classified as enum instance `NOT_MEMBER_OF`, and implicit-invoke recovery rejected explicit receivers before constructing its temporary value access. A later heuristic inspected `callKind`/`callSite`, but enum fallback had already changed the temporary call kind to `EnumConstructorCall`, so the heuristic could not identify the receiver probe.
- official Cangjie evidence: cjc 1.0.5 reports only `sema_invalid_enum_member_access` for `value.Year`, only `sema_no_match_operator_function_call` for zero-payload `value.Year()`, and invalid enum member access for payload constructor calls. Implementation evidence was traced through `external/cangjie_compiler/src/Parse/ParseDecl.cpp:1194,1237`, `Sema/TypeCheckExpr/NameReferenceExpr.cpp:1064`, `TypeCheckReference.cpp:635`, `TypeCheckCall.cpp:2536,2839,3029`, and `DesugarInTypeCheck.cpp:481`.
- Kotlin counterpart files consulted: `external/kotlin/compiler/fir/resolve/src/org/jetbrains/kotlin/fir/resolve/calls/FirCallResolver.kt`, `calls/candidate/CallInfo.kt`, `calls/tower/FirInvokeResolveTowerExtension.kt`, and `calls/stages/ResolutionStages.kt` for explicit implicit-invoke call/receiver state rather than call-site shape heuristics.
- CFIR owner files changed: `cfir/resolve/src/org/cangnova/cangjie/cfir/resolve/body/CfirCallResolver.kt` and `CfirExpressionsResolveTransformer.kt`.
- repair principle: carry an explicit `ImplicitInvokeReceiver` purpose through named-value resolution, normalize only a zero-payload enum constructor on a runtime enum receiver to a temporary enum value, and let the existing invoke lookup own the final failure.
- fixtures covered: PSI and non-PSI `enum2.cj`; guards included `enum1.cj` and `enum1_optionalBITOR.cj` for type-qualified zero-payload calls.
- fixture correction: none.
- verification commands: focused `.\gradlew.bat :cfir:analysis-tests:test --rerun --tests '*EnumGenerated.testEnum2' ...`; then the clean combined guard command selecting `testEnum2`, `testEnum1`, and `testEnum1OptionalBITOR` on both entry paths.
- verification outcome: focused `enum2` passed 2/2 with `BUILD SUCCESSFUL in 7m 22s`; the clean combined guard set passed with `BUILD SUCCESSFUL in 3m 50s`.

## Declared generic upper bounds use strict subtype relations

- problem type: use-site validation of a declared generic upper bound must not treat expression-level `T -> Option<T>` promotion as satisfying the bound; an illegal enum upper-bound declaration still participates in explicit type-argument constraint checking.
- root cause: fresh-variable initialization correctly added `α <: Option<B>` and explicit `test<B>()` added `α == B`, but constraint incorporation checked the derived proper constraint `B <: Option<B>` with the ordinary subtype state, whose Option auto-boxing returned true. The post-resolve upper-bound checker repeated the same permissive comparison, so no solver or checker diagnostic was produced.
- official Cangjie evidence: cjc 1.0.5 reports both `sema_upper_bound_must_be_class_or_interface` on `T` and `sema_generic_type_argument_not_match_constraint` on explicit `B` in `enum26_1.cj`. `external/cangjie_compiler/src/Sema/PreCheck.cpp:1036-1057` diagnoses the illegal bound kind without disabling later use-site checking, and `TypeCheckGeneric.cpp:250-275` validates the actual type argument against the declared bound.
- Kotlin counterpart files consulted: `external/kotlin/compiler/fir/resolve/src/org/jetbrains/kotlin/fir/resolve/calls/stages/CreateFreshTypeVariableSubstitutorStage.kt`, `external/kotlin/compiler/fir/checkers/src/org/jetbrains/kotlin/fir/analysis/checkers/FirUpperBoundViolatedHelpers.kt`, `FirUpperBoundViolatedQualifiedAccessExpressionChecker.kt`, and `FirUpperBoundViolatedTypeChecker.kt`; solver and checker consume the same resolved bounds.
- CFIR owner files changed: `common/src/org/cangnova/cangjie/type/AbstractTypeChecker.kt`; `resolution.common/src/org/cangnova/cangjie/resolve/calls/inference/components/ConstraintInjector.kt`; `cfir/resolve/src/org/cangnova/cangjie/cfir/resolve/calls/stages/CfirCreateFreshTypeVariableSubstitutorStage.kt`; `cfir/resolve/src/org/cangnova/cangjie/cfir/resolve/inference/CfirCallCompleter.kt`; `cfir/providers/src/org/cangnova/cangjie/cfir/resolve/providers/CfirExtendSubstitution.kt`; `cfir/checkers/src/org/cangnova/cangjie/cfir/analysis/checkers/CfirUpperBoundViolatedHelpers.kt`; `CfirGenericInstantiationChecker.kt`; and `coneDiagnosticToCfirDiagnostic.kt`.
- repair principle: expose one shared subtype entry that disables only Option promotion, use it for every declared-bound solver/checker comparison, and retain ordinary Option promotion for expression target typing.
- fixtures covered: PSI and non-PSI `enum26_1.cj`, `err_invalid_upper.cj`, and `generic_constraint11.cj`; `optional_chain/member_access_02.cj` and `member_access_03.cj` guarded ordinary Option behavior. The wider guard run also showed `generic_constraint5.cj` has only an independent `AMBIGUOUS_USE` range mismatch, while `generic_constraint14.cj` remains a separate nested static-generic-qualifier resolution problem.
- fixture correction: none.
- verification commands: focused six-test command selecting `testEnum`, `testEnum2`, and `testEnum261`; then the clean combined guard command selecting `testEnum261`, `testErrInvalidUpper`, `testGenericConstraint11`, `testMemberAccess02`, and `testMemberAccess03` on PSI/LT entry paths.
- verification outcome: `enum26_1` passed in the six-test run; the clean combined guard set passed with `BUILD SUCCESSFUL in 3m 50s`.

## Concrete generic type-pattern reachability without Option promotion

- problem type: concrete sealed-like generic types with the same classifier but different invariant arguments must be recognized as disjoint type patterns; an unreachable type-pattern row must not cover a later wildcard.
- root cause: `AbstractTypeChecker` performed Option auto-boxing before same-constructor invariant argument comparison, so `Option<Int64> <: Option<Option<Int64>>` incorrectly returned true. `convertPattern` therefore downgraded the first type pattern to a wildcard before Maranget analysis, added it to `previousRows`, and made the following wildcard unreachable.
- official Cangjie evidence: cjc 1.0.5 reports `sema_unreachable_pattern` on `case _: Option<Option<Int64>>` for an `Option<Int64>` selector while the following `_` remains reachable. `external/cangjie_compiler/src/Sema/PatternUsefulness.cpp:92-123,469-511,603-613,847-862,1020-1053` computes a witness intersection and does not add a witness-less row to the preceding matrix.
- Kotlin counterpart files consulted: `external/kotlin/compiler/fir/resolve/src/org/jetbrains/kotlin/fir/resolve/transformers/FirWhenExhaustivenessComputer.kt` and `FirControlFlowStatementsResolveTransformer.kt`; branch coverage is accumulated from the actual subject-domain intersection rather than every syntactic branch.
- CFIR owner files changed: `common/src/org/cangnova/cangjie/type/AbstractTypeChecker.kt`; `cfir/semantics/src/org/cangnova/cangjie/cfir/resolve/match/CfirMatchTypeRelations.kt`; `cfir/semantics/src/org/cangnova/cangjie/cfir/resolve/match/exhaustive/inria/MarangetChecker.kt`; and `cfir/checkers/src/org/cangnova/cangjie/cfir/analysis/checkers/expression/CfirMatchUnreachablePatternChecker.kt`.
- repair principle: use the strict no-Option-promotion subtype relation at every type-pattern ordinary-subtype seam, treat only unresolved generic components as open shapes, and add only reachable unguarded rows to coverage state.
- fixtures covered: PSI and non-PSI `enum.cj`; guards included `PatternMatching/Sealed/exhaustive_interface.cj`, `TypePattern/unreachable_simple.cj`, `unreachable_nothing.cj`, `type11.cj`, `exhaustive_generic.cj`, and `Sealed/sealed_open_unreachable.cj`. The wider guard run identified an independent diagnostic-range mismatch in `non_exhaustive_generic.cj` and pre-existing path-specific issues in `match024.cj`.
- fixture correction: none.
- verification commands: focused six-test command selecting `testEnum`, `testEnum2`, and `testEnum261`; then the clean combined guard command selecting the six listed type-pattern guards on both PSI/LT entry paths.
- verification outcome: `enum.cj` passed in the six-test run; the clean combined guard set passed with `BUILD SUCCESSFUL in 3m 50s`.

## Internal unused-import checking follows no-sub-package compilation mode

- problem type: `internal import` participates in unused-import diagnostics only when the source package is compiled with `--no-sub-pkg`; when it participates, usage is collected across the whole package rather than only the importing file.
- root cause: CFIR treated every non-public/protected `internal import` as an unused-import candidate regardless of package compilation mode. The checker already had package-wide usage collection for internal imports, but the configuration and session layers did not carry the official `noSubPkg` mode, so default-mode imports such as `enum43_import.cj` were falsely reported unused.
- official Cangjie evidence: cjc 1.0.5 reports no diagnostics for `enum43_import.cj` by default and reports one `sema_unused_import` for `std.convert.*` with `--no-sub-pkg`. `external/cangjie_compiler/src/Option/OptionAction.cpp:659` sets the option, `src/Frontend/CompilerInstance.cpp:306-313` writes it to the source package, `include/cangjie/AST/Node.h:2847-2853` defines re-export behavior by `noSubPkg`, and `src/Sema/CheckUnusedImportImpl.cpp:87-112,139-173,210-286` excludes public/protected imports, excludes internal imports only when `noSubPkg` is false, and selects package-wide usage for non-private imports. `src/Parse/ParseImports.cpp:113-163` explains the per-item diagnostics for grouped imports.
- Kotlin counterpart files consulted: `external/kotlin/compiler/fir/checkers/src/org/jetbrains/kotlin/fir/analysis/checkers/declaration/FirImportsChecker.kt`, `external/kotlin/compiler/fir/resolve/src/org/jetbrains/kotlin/fir/resolve/transformers/FirImportResolveTransformer.kt`, and `external/kotlin/compiler/fir/entrypoint/src/org/jetbrains/kotlin/fir/session/FirAbstractSessionFactory.kt`; Kotlin compiler has no unused-import diagnostic, so its IDE `K2OptimizeImportsFacility.kt` and `KotlinUnusedImportInspection.kt` were consulted only for ownership separation, while the session setting-holder pattern came from `FirLookupDefaultStarImportsInSourcesSettingHolder.kt`.
- CFIR owner files changed: `cfir/entrypoint/src/org/cangnova/cangjie/cfir/entrypoint/configuration/CfirFrontendConfigurationKeys.kt`, `cfir/cfir-tree/src/org/cangnova/cangjie/cfir/session/CfirLanguageSettingsComponent.kt`, `cfir/entrypoint/src/org/cangnova/cangjie/cfir/entrypoint/session/CfirAbstractSessionFactory.kt`, `tests/test-infrastructure/testFixtures/org/cangnova/cangjie/test/directives/CfirDiagnosticsDirectives.kt`, `tests/test-infrastructure/testFixtures/org/cangnova/cangjie/test/services/EnvironmentConfigurator.kt`, `cfir/providers/src/org/cangnova/cangjie/cfir/resolve/providers/CfirImportReexportInfo.kt`, and `cfir/checkers/src/org/cangnova/cangjie/cfir/analysis/checkers/declaration/CfirImportsChecker.kt`; fixture corrections add `// NO_SUB_PKG` to all files under `unusedImport003` and `unusedImport004`.
- repair principle: carry package compilation mode through the shared configuration-to-session chain and let the import semantic helper decide unused-check exemption, while preserving internal import's same-package visibility and package-wide usage accounting in both modes.
- fixtures covered: PSI and non-PSI `enum43_import.cj`; `unusedImport003/unused001.cj` through `unused009.cj`; and `unusedImport004/unused001.cj` through `unused009.cj`.
- fixture correction: all 18 `unusedImport003`/`unusedImport004` fixtures now explicitly select `NO_SUB_PKG`, because official cjc proves their internal-unused expectations are valid only in that mode; `enum43_import.cj` remains in the default mode.
- focused verification command: `.\gradlew.bat :cfir:analysis-tests:test --rerun --tests '*EnumGenerated.testEnum43Import' --tests '*unusedImport003.UnusedImport003Generated' --tests '*unusedImport004.UnusedImport004Generated' -x :cfir:analysis-tests:generateTestGeneratorForCfirAnalysisTestsTests --no-configuration-cache --no-daemon --max-workers=1 --console=plain`.
- focused verification outcome: `BUILD SUCCESSFUL in 12m 50s`; XML showed 42 tests, 0 failures, and 0 errors across PSI and LightTree, including four `allFilesPresent` metadata tests.
- complete Enum verification command: `.\gradlew.bat :cfir:analysis-tests:test --rerun --tests '*llt.enum.EnumGenerated*' -x :cfir:analysis-tests:generateTestGeneratorForCfirAnalysisTestsTests --no-configuration-cache --no-daemon --max-workers=1 --console=plain`.
- complete Enum verification outcome: `BUILD SUCCESSFUL in 2m 18s`; XML showed 142 tests, 0 failures, and 0 errors.
- full LLT regression command: `.\gradlew.bat :cfir:analysis-tests:test --rerun -x :cfir:analysis-tests:generateTestGeneratorForCfirAnalysisTestsTests --no-configuration-cache --no-daemon --max-workers=1 --console=plain`.
- full LLT regression outcome: the clean single-worker run did not complete because the independent PSI test `CfirAnalysisLLTPsiTest.llt.operator_overload.OperatorOverloadGenerated.testTimeout06` entered an unbounded high-CPU traversal. A `jcmd Thread.print` dump showed repeated `CfirLocalVariableAssignmentAnalyzer.MiniCfgBuilder.visitFunctionCall` frames at lines 479/589/590 while visiting the unary-operator call graph. The process was stopped after the hang was proven; this operator-overload/DFA traversal issue is the next problem type and does not invalidate the focused or complete Enum verification above.

## Shared receiver DAG evaluation in local-assignment mini CFG

- problem type: deeply nested resolved unary-operator calls must be traversed linearly by the local-assignment mini CFG even when each call stores the same operand object in both explicit and dispatch receiver slots.
- root cause: `MiniCfgBuilder.visitFunctionCall` unconditionally visited `explicitReceiver` and `dispatchReceiver`. Completed unary calls legitimately share one CFIR receiver DAG node between those slots, so a chain of roughly 64 unary calls expanded as `T(n) = 2*T(n-1)` and kept the PSI `timeout_06` test at high CPU for hours.
- official Cangjie evidence: cjc 1.0.5 compiled `timeout_06.cj` successfully with 0 diagnostics in 1393 ms. `external/cangjie_compiler/src/Sema/TypeCheckCall.cpp:1365-1408,1596-1608,1960-2011,2096-2143` checks an untyped lambda separately against each overload candidate with suppressed diagnostics; `src/Sema/TypeCheckExpr/LambdaExpr.cpp:311-405` injects the candidate function type and safely rechecks the lambda; `src/Sema/TypeCheckExpr/UnaryExpr.cpp:73-98` and `src/Sema/Desugar/DesugarInTypeCheck.cpp:322-344` process each unary node once and reuse an existing desugared call on re-entry.
- Kotlin counterpart files consulted: `external/kotlin/compiler/fir/resolve/src/org/jetbrains/kotlin/fir/resolve/dfa/FirLocalVariableAssignmentAnalyzer.kt`, whose `MiniCfgBuilder.visitFunctionCall` deduplicates explicit, dispatch, and extension receivers before visiting; Kotlin generated `FirFunctionCallImpl.kt` and CFIR generated `CfirFunctionCallImpl.kt` also guard receiver traversal by object identity.
- CFIR owner files changed: `cfir/resolve/src/org/cangnova/cangjie/cfir/resolve/dfa/CfirLocalVariableAssignmentAnalyzer.kt`.
- repair principle: preserve the custom call evaluation order while evaluating each shared receiver DAG node exactly once by object identity; do not add a global visited set that could hide illegal cycles or discard context-sensitive flow traversal.
- fixtures covered: PSI and non-PSI `timeout_00.cj`, `timeout_01.cj`, `timeout_03.cj`, `timeout_04.cj`, `timeout_06.cj`, `timeout_07.cj`, plus `operator/unary_overload.cj` as the ordinary unary-overload guard.
- fixture correction: none.
- family verification command: `.\gradlew.bat :cfir:analysis-tests:test --rerun --tests '*operator_overload.OperatorOverloadGenerated.testTimeout*' --tests '*operator.OperatorGenerated.testUnaryOverload' -x :cfir:analysis-tests:generateTestGeneratorForCfirAnalysisTestsTests --no-configuration-cache --no-daemon --max-workers=1 --console=plain`.
- family verification outcome: all 14 PSI/LightTree tests completed in `6m 58s`, proving the hang was removed. `timeout_03`, `timeout_04`, `timeout_06`, and `unary_overload` passed on both entry paths; only the pre-existing expectation mismatches in `timeout_00`, `timeout_01`, and `timeout_07` failed on both paths.
- focused verification command: `.\gradlew.bat :cfir:analysis-tests:test --rerun --tests '*operator_overload.OperatorOverloadGenerated.testTimeout06' -x :cfir:analysis-tests:generateTestGeneratorForCfirAnalysisTestsTests --no-configuration-cache --no-daemon --max-workers=1 --console=plain`.
- focused verification outcome: `BUILD SUCCESSFUL in 1m 40s`; both PSI and LightTree `timeout_06` tests passed.

## Nested return-type overload refinement reuses immutable tower discovery

- problem type: deeply nested calls whose overloads share the same parameter shape but differ by concrete return type must be refined by the outer parameter type without replaying full tower discovery at every nesting level.
- root cause: `ArgumentCheckingProcessor.resolveNestedCallForExpectedType` rebuilt a copied call and invoked the complete tower resolver for every outer candidate and every nested level. Although an early expected-return stage stopped incompatible candidates before their arguments, the root call still created eight candidate chains, each level rediscovered the same overload symbols, and `CfirResolutionSnapshot` repeatedly traversed the remaining nested subtree. Concrete primitive subtype checks also entered the general corresponding-supertype BFS before the shared type system recognized that different primitive kinds are final and unrelated.
- official Cangjie evidence: cjc 1.0.5 compiles `overload_resolution_2.cj` with zero diagnostics in about 1128 ms. `external/cangjie_compiler/src/Sema/TypeCheckCall.cpp:1165-1198,1365-1408,1957-2011,2096-2143` discovers the function declaration list once, checks each `FunctionCandidate` with candidate-local constraint checkpoints and the target return type, then reapplies only the selected candidate's argument inference. `external/cangjie_compiler/src/Sema/TypeManager.cpp:958-990,1000+` handles ordinary primitive subtype relations by exact primitive kind before interface/extend traversal, with separate rules for ideal literals and `Nothing`.
- Kotlin counterpart files consulted: `external/kotlin/compiler/fir/resolve/src/org/jetbrains/kotlin/fir/resolve/calls/stages/ArgumentCheckingProcessor.kt`, `ConeResolutionAtoms.kt`, `candidate/CandidateFactory.kt`, `FirCallResolver.kt`, and `inference/FirCallCompleter.kt`; Kotlin keeps nested candidate atoms and subsystem storage, separates tower discovery from fresh candidate state, and applies the outer expected type through the candidate/completion pipeline rather than redoing name lookup.
- CFIR owner files changed: `common/src/org/cangnova/cangjie/type/AbstractTypeChecker.kt`; `cfir/resolve/src/org/cangnova/cangjie/cfir/resolve/calls/ExpectedTypeCompatibility.kt`; `calls/candidate/CfirCallableCandidateDiscovery.kt`; `CfirCandidateCollector.kt`; `CandidateFactory.kt`; `CallKind.kt`; `calls/stages/CfirCheckExpectedReturnTypeBeforeArguments.kt`; `ArgumentCheckingProcessor.kt`; and `body/CfirCallResolver.kt`.
- repair principle: keep tower discovery as immutable call-node metadata, deduplicate the same declaration discovered through equivalent file/package scopes, and create a completely fresh candidate only when all return shapes are deterministic and the outer expected type selects a unique target; never reuse an old candidate's system, stage progress, diagnostics, or replacements.
- fixtures covered: PSI and non-PSI `overload_resolution_2.cj`; `overload_resolution_1.cj` through `overload_resolution_5.cj` were run as the neighboring overload guard family.
- fixture correction: none.
- focused verification command: `.\gradlew.bat :cfir:analysis-tests:test --rerun --tests '*overload.OverloadGenerated.testOverloadResolution2' -x :cfir:analysis-tests:generateTestGeneratorForCfirAnalysisTestsTests --no-configuration-cache --no-daemon --max-workers=1 --console=plain`.
- focused verification outcome: `BUILD SUCCESSFUL in 4m 16s`; XML recorded PSI `testOverloadResolution2` in 7.174 s and LightTree in 1.044 s, both with zero failures and errors. The previous 15-minute high-CPU blocker no longer reproduces.
- family verification command: `.\gradlew.bat :cfir:analysis-tests:test --rerun --tests '*overload.OverloadGenerated.testOverloadResolution*' -x :cfir:analysis-tests:generateTestGeneratorForCfirAnalysisTestsTests --no-configuration-cache --no-daemon --max-workers=1 --console=plain`.
- family verification outcome: all 10 PSI/LightTree tests completed in 1m15s. `overload_resolution_2`, `_4`, and `_5` passed on both entry paths; `_1` has an independent diagnostic-range mismatch (`ConsTest.f` versus selector-only `f`), and `_3` has an independent unresolved function-value invocation in constructor bodies. Neither remaining family failure is a timeout or nested expected-return refinement failure.

## Expected-return discovery is body-resolve transaction state

- problem type: immutable overload discovery retained for nested expected-return refinement must not keep completed call trees alive for the entire CFIR session.
- root cause: `CfirCallResolver` is session-owned through the body-resolve processor, while its identity map strongly retained call nodes and discovery values containing symbol, scope, and receiver graphs; no transaction boundary cleared the map after a file body finished resolving.
- official Cangjie evidence: this is a CFIR lifecycle correction rather than a language-semantic divergence; the already recorded `TypeCheckCall.cpp:1957-2011,2096-2143` evidence establishes candidate-local reuse only within one call-check transaction, not a compiler-session cache.
- Kotlin counterpart files consulted: `external/kotlin/compiler/fir/resolve/src/org/jetbrains/kotlin/fir/resolve/calls/FirCallResolver.kt`, `calls/candidate/CandidateFactory.kt`, and `transformers/body/resolve/FirExpressionsResolveTransformer.kt`; candidate/discovery state is owned by the active resolution transaction and is not stored as an unbounded session map.
- CFIR owner files changed: `cfir/resolve/src/org/cangnova/cangjie/cfir/resolve/body/CfirCallResolver.kt` and `CfirAbstractBodyResolveTransformer.kt`.
- repair principle: keep identity-based discovery alive for nested re-resolution inside one file body transaction, then clear it unconditionally in the outer `transformFile` `finally` boundary; do not replace it with a weak-key map whose values can retain the keys through scope/symbol graphs.
- fixtures covered: PSI and non-PSI `overload_resolution_2.cj`; the lifecycle boundary applies to every body-resolved file.
- fixture correction: none.
- verification command: `.\gradlew.bat :cfir:analysis-tests:test --tests "org.cangnova.cangjie.cfir.analysis.tests.CfirAnalysisLLTTest.llt.overload.OverloadGenerated.testOverloadResolution2" --tests "org.cangnova.cangjie.cfir.analysis.tests.CfirAnalysisLLTPsiTest.llt.overload.OverloadGenerated.testOverloadResolution2" -x :cfir:analysis-tests:generateTestGeneratorForCfirAnalysisTestsTests --no-configuration-cache --no-daemon --max-workers=1 --console=plain`.
- verification outcome: `BUILD SUCCESSFUL in 41s`; both PSI and LightTree focused tests passed after transaction cleanup.

## Variable-backed function values use the shared implicit-invoke path

- problem type: function values stored in enum payloads and recovered through match bindings must be callable from the branch body, just like parameters and ordinary local variables.
- root cause: the match binding was correctly registered and typed as `(Int32) -> Int32`, but the first direct-call attempt produced `ConeInapplicableCandidateError` for a `CfirPatternBindingSymbol`. Implicit-invoke recovery accepted that diagnostic only for enum constructors, and its declaration-type recovery was limited to `CfirValueParameterSymbol`, so variable-backed function values were left as `UNRESOLVED_REFERENCE`.
- official Cangjie evidence: cjc 1.0.5 compiles `enum11_1.cj` successfully (exit 0; only the independent unused-parameter warning). `external/cangjie_compiler/src/Sema/TypeCheckCall.cpp:2536-2578,2839-2890,2908-2989` classifies non-declaration call bases separately and invokes `CheckFuncPtrCall` whenever the base expression type can be cast to `FuncTy`.
- Kotlin counterpart files consulted: `external/kotlin/compiler/fir/resolve/src/org/jetbrains/kotlin/fir/resolve/calls/FirCallResolver.kt:120-145`, `calls/tower/FirInvokeResolveTowerExtension.kt:130-218`, and `calls/candidate/CallInfo.kt`; Kotlin first resolves any callable receiver candidate, calculates its return type, then creates a common implicit-`invoke` call instead of restricting receiver ownership to value parameters or enum entries.
- CFIR owner files changed: `cfir/resolve/src/org/cangnova/cangjie/cfir/resolve/body/CfirExpressionsResolveTransformer.kt` and `CfirCallResolver.kt`.
- repair principle: classify every `CfirVariableSymbol` as a valid implicit-invoke receiver candidate, recover the declaration-side type through the shared variable-symbol abstraction, and keep enum-pattern constructor probing diagnostic-free when the bare name is actually a binding.
- fixtures covered: all PSI and non-PSI `llt/enum/EnumGenerated` fixtures were run; the previously failing `enum11_1.cj` and neighboring function-value cases now pass. Only `enum25_3.cj` remains failing on each entry path, with an independent expectation mismatch.
- fixture correction: none.
- focused verification command: `.\gradlew.bat :cfir:analysis-tests:test --tests "org.cangnova.cangjie.cfir.analysis.tests.CfirAnalysisLLTTest.llt.enum.EnumGenerated.testEnum111" --tests "org.cangnova.cangjie.cfir.analysis.tests.CfirAnalysisLLTPsiTest.llt.enum.EnumGenerated.testEnum111" -x :cfir:analysis-tests:generateTestGeneratorForCfirAnalysisTestsTests --no-configuration-cache --no-daemon --max-workers=1 --console=plain`.
- focused verification outcome: `BUILD SUCCESSFUL in 3m 36s`; both PSI and LightTree `enum11_1` tests passed.
- family verification command: `.\gradlew.bat :cfir:analysis-tests:test --tests "org.cangnova.cangjie.cfir.analysis.tests.CfirAnalysisLLTTest.llt.enum.EnumGenerated" --tests "org.cangnova.cangjie.cfir.analysis.tests.CfirAnalysisLLTPsiTest.llt.enum.EnumGenerated" -x :cfir:analysis-tests:generateTestGeneratorForCfirAnalysisTestsTests --no-configuration-cache --no-daemon --max-workers=1 --console=plain`.
- family verification outcome: 138 tests completed in 1m 2s; 136 passed and only PSI/LightTree `enum25_3` failed. This reduces the Enum suite from the prior 19 failures per entry path to one independent failure per entry path.

## Nested functions in member initializers preserve object-initialization semantics

- problem type: a local function or its default parameter declared inside an instance-field initializer must not access current-class or inherited instance members before object initialization completes.
- root cause: the shared initialization analyzer entered nested function bodies but skipped value-parameter defaults, classified every nested access as a local capture, and required the individual field to still be uninitialized. Resolution also failed to prepare signatures for functions syntactically nested in class member initializers and did not register such local functions because the outer container was a class, causing value-parameter return-type exceptions and unresolved sibling local-function references.
- official Cangjie evidence: cjc 1.0.5 reports `sema_illegal_usage_of_member` for the five current-class accesses in `variable_use_before_init.cj` and the struct access in `capture_func_record_3601.cj`; it reports `sema_illegal_usage_of_super_member` for both default-parameter accesses in `localfuncinvarinit.cj` and the inherited access in `capture_super_member.cj`. `external/cangjie_compiler/src/Sema/LegalityOfUsage/InitializationChecker.cpp:413-428,1481-1548` checks parameter defaults and forbids member/super-member capture while the object is being initialized.
- Kotlin counterpart files consulted: `external/kotlin/compiler/fir/checkers/src/org/jetbrains/kotlin/fir/analysis/cfa/VariableInitializationCheckProcessor.kt`, `FirPropertyInitializationAnalyzer.kt`, `analysis/cfa/util/PropertyInitializationInfoCollector.kt`, and `external/kotlin/compiler/fir/resolve/src/org/jetbrains/kotlin/fir/resolve/dfa/cfg/ControlFlowGraphBuilder.kt`; Kotlin keeps nested graph ownership and member-initializer evaluation context in shared CFA rather than treating every nested access as the same capture kind.
- CFIR owner files changed: `cfir/checkers/src/org/cangnova/cangjie/cfir/analysis/checkers/declaration/CfirInitializationCheckers.kt`; `cfir/resolve/src/org/cangnova/cangjie/cfir/resolve/body/CfirDeclarationsResolveTransformer.kt`; `cfir/resolve/src/org/cangnova/cangjie/cfir/resolve/transformers/body/resolve/BodyResolveContext.kt`.
- repair principle: classify tracked declarations by semantic storage kind, analyze nested defaults before the body, preserve member-initializer object state across nested functions, and use declaration locality—not merely the outer container kind—to prepare and register local function signatures.
- fixtures covered: PSI and non-PSI `localfuncinvarinit.cj`, `variable_use_before_init.cj`, `capture_func_record_3601.cj`, and `capture_super_member.cj` (18 expected diagnostic points across both entry paths).
- fixture correction: none.
- focused verification command: `.\gradlew.bat :cfir:analysis-tests:test --tests "*InitializationCheckGenerated.testLocalfuncinvarinit" --tests "*InitializationCheckGenerated.testVariableUseBeforeInit" --tests "*InitializationCheckGenerated.testCaptureFuncRecord3601" --tests "*InitializationCheckGenerated.testCaptureSuperMember" -x :cfir:analysis-tests:generateTestGeneratorForCfirAnalysisTestsTests --no-configuration-cache --no-daemon --max-workers=1 --console=plain`.
- focused verification outcome: `BUILD SUCCESSFUL in 52s`; all 8 PSI/LightTree tests passed.
- class verification command: `.\gradlew.bat :cfir:analysis-tests:test --tests "org.cangnova.cangjie.cfir.analysis.tests.CfirAnalysisLLTTest.llt.InitializationCheck.InitializationCheckGenerated" --tests "org.cangnova.cangjie.cfir.analysis.tests.CfirAnalysisLLTPsiTest.llt.InitializationCheck.InitializationCheckGenerated" -x :cfir:analysis-tests:generateTestGeneratorForCfirAnalysisTestsTests --no-configuration-cache --no-daemon --max-workers=1 --console=plain`.
- class verification outcome: 80 tests completed in 43s; failures dropped from 19 to 15 per entry path. The remaining 15 per path are the independently evidenced constructor-return, try/throw, loop-jump, multiple-assignment, visibility, global dependency, named-argument, capture-priority, and tuple-pattern subclusters.

## Constructor completeness checks every normal return exit

- problem type: an instance constructor that returns before all required fields are initialized must be rejected even when another branch or the lexical function end initializes the fields.
- root cause: initialization analysis collapsed control flow to one end state. A `return` only set `terminated = true`, branch merge discarded that terminated path, and constructor completeness skipped a terminated end state entirely, so early-return exits never participated in definite assignment.
- official Cangjie evidence: cjc 1.0.5 reports `sema_class_uninitialized_field` for both constructors in `variable_assignment_terminated_in_ctor_01.cj` and only the conditionally returning first constructor in `_02.cj`. `external/cangjie_compiler/src/Sema/LegalityOfUsage/InitializationChecker.cpp:313-350,1600-1657` records the uninitialized-member snapshot at each constructor `return` and combines it with the ordinary body exit.
- Kotlin counterpart files consulted: `external/kotlin/compiler/fir/checkers/src/org/jetbrains/kotlin/fir/analysis/checkers/declaration/FirMemberPropertiesChecker.kt`, `analysis/cfa/util/PropertyInitializationInfoCollector.kt`, and `VariableInitializationCheckProcessor.kt`; constructor completeness consumes every relevant CFG normal exit rather than a single lexical end state.
- CFIR owner files changed: `cfir/checkers/src/org/cangnova/cangjie/cfir/analysis/checkers/declaration/CfirInitializationCheckers.kt`.
- repair principle: retain reachable explicit-return initialization snapshots by target-function identity, combine them with a non-terminated lexical end, and report each field once when any constructor exit leaves it uninitialized; nested-function returns remain outside the constructor exit set.
- fixtures covered: PSI and non-PSI `variable_assignment_terminated_in_ctor_01.cj` and `variable_assignment_terminated_in_ctor_02.cj`.
- fixture correction: none.
- focused verification command: `.\gradlew.bat :cfir:analysis-tests:test --tests "*InitializationCheckGenerated.testVariableAssignmentTerminatedInCtor01" --tests "*InitializationCheckGenerated.testVariableAssignmentTerminatedInCtor02" -x :cfir:analysis-tests:generateTestGeneratorForCfirAnalysisTestsTests --no-configuration-cache --no-daemon --max-workers=1 --console=plain --quiet`.
- focused verification outcome: all 4 PSI/LightTree tests passed.
- class verification command: `.\gradlew.bat :cfir:analysis-tests:test --tests "org.cangnova.cangjie.cfir.analysis.tests.CfirAnalysisLLTTest.llt.InitializationCheck.InitializationCheckGenerated" --tests "org.cangnova.cangjie.cfir.analysis.tests.CfirAnalysisLLTPsiTest.llt.InitializationCheck.InitializationCheckGenerated" -x :cfir:analysis-tests:generateTestGeneratorForCfirAnalysisTestsTests --no-configuration-cache --no-daemon --max-workers=1 --console=plain --quiet`.
- class verification outcome: 80 tests completed in 40s; failures dropped from 15 to 13 per entry path.

## Multiple assignment owns tuple compatibility and suppresses derived condition errors

- problem type: tuple multiple assignment must evaluate the complete RHS before writes, initialize every target represented by the desugared assignments, report only the first tuple shape/type incompatibility on the complete RHS, and prevent that root error from cascading into an outer `if`/loop condition mismatch.
- root cause: initialization analysis treated tuple lvalues as reads and continued traversing sibling operands after the first initialization diagnostic; resolve then modeled every assignment as a successful `Unit` expression, while the ordinary assignment checker compared the two tuple types as one normal assignment. This produced a left-side `Bool <- Unit` condition mismatch plus a right-side `ASSIGNMENT_TYPE_MISMATCH`, with overlapping ranges, instead of one multiple-assignment root diagnostic.
- official Cangjie evidence: cjc 1.0.5 reports exactly one `sema_mismatched_types_multiple_assign` on each `(1, 2, 3)` RHS in `variable_use_before_init_03.cj`, and that mismatch plus only the first later uninitialized `b` in `_04.cj`; `_02.cj` reports only its three first reachable uninitialized `b` reads. `external/cangjie_compiler/src/Sema/TypeCheckExpr/AssignExpr.cpp:93-131,310-325` performs recursive tuple shape/type checking with first-error return and suppresses diagnostics from synthesizing the original tuple lvalue; `TypeCheckExpr/Diags.cpp:12-18` anchors the primary diagnostic on the RHS and keeps the LHS as a secondary hint; `Desugar/DesugarBeforeTypeCheck.cpp:309-345` evaluates the whole RHS into a temporary before left-to-right writes; `LegalityOfUsage/InitializationChecker.cpp:862-909,1005-1007,1326-1333` analyzes the desugared writes and short-circuits tuple/binary initialization diagnostics after the first failure.
- Kotlin counterpart files consulted: Kotlin FIR `FirExpressionsResolveTransformer.kt`, `FirAssignmentTypeMismatchChecker.kt`, `FirHelpers.checkTypeMismatch`, `FirDataFlowAnalyzer.kt`, `FirDestructuringDeclarationChecker.kt`, and `ErrorNodeDiagnosticCollectorComponent.kt`; assignment compatibility has one diagnostic owner, existing error types suppress derived mismatches, and destructuring-level errors dominate lower-level entry errors.
- CFIR owner files changed: `cfir/checkers/src/org/cangnova/cangjie/cfir/analysis/checkers/declaration/CfirInitializationCheckers.kt`; `cfir/resolve/src/org/cangnova/cangjie/cfir/resolve/body/CfirExpressionsResolveTransformer.kt`; `cfir/semantics/src/org/cangnova/cangjie/cfir/diagnostic/ConeDiagnostic.kt`; `cfir/checkers/src/org/cangnova/cangjie/cfir/analysis/checkers/expression/CfirAssignmentTypeMismatchChecker.kt`; `CfirIfConditionTypeMismatchChecker.kt`; `CfirLoopConditionTypeMismatchChecker.kt`; and `cfir/checkers/src/org/cangnova/cangjie/cfir/analysis/diagnostics/coneDiagnosticToCfirDiagnostic.kt`.
- repair principle: classify tuple shape, arity, and component compatibility once at the shared assignment seam, retain the root as a structured duplicate error owned by the assignment checker, report the project `TYPE_MISMATCH` only on the RHS, and let all outer condition checkers suppress any existing error type without tuple-specific syntax branches.
- fixtures covered: PSI and non-PSI `variable_use_before_init_02.cj`, `variable_use_before_init_03.cj`, and `variable_use_before_init_04.cj`; the compatibility classifier also covers nested tuples, discard targets, scalar RHS values, function-returned tuples, and first incompatible components.
- fixture correction: none.
- focused verification command: `.\gradlew.bat :cfir:analysis-tests:test --tests '*.InitializationCheckGenerated.testVariableUseBeforeInit02' --tests '*.InitializationCheckGenerated.testVariableUseBeforeInit03' --tests '*.InitializationCheckGenerated.testVariableUseBeforeInit04' -x :cfir:analysis-tests:generateTestGeneratorForCfirAnalysisTestsTests --no-configuration-cache --no-daemon --max-workers=1 --console=plain`.
- focused verification outcome: `BUILD SUCCESSFUL in 4m 19s`; all 6 PSI/LightTree tests passed. An earlier identical source build was interrupted by a Windows class-directory lock, and the first successful compilation confirmed no source errors.
- class verification command: `.\gradlew.bat :cfir:analysis-tests:test --tests '*.InitializationCheck.InitializationCheckGenerated' -x :cfir:analysis-tests:generateTestGeneratorForCfirAnalysisTestsTests --no-configuration-cache --no-daemon --max-workers=1 --console=plain`.
- class verification outcome: 80 tests completed in 52s; 18 failed, reducing the remaining InitializationCheck slice from 13 to 9 independent failures per entry path.

## Explicit Unit function bodies do not constrain ordinary tail-call results

- problem type: an ordinary function explicitly returning `Unit` may contain a generic call as its final statement, but the declaration return type must not be propagated as the expected result type of that call or its postponed lambda.
- root cause: `CfirDeclarationsResolveTransformer.transformFunctionContent` converted every explicit function return type into the body resolution mode. For `main(): Unit { f2(1) { v => [v] } }`, this forced the outer generic result `R` to `Unit` before lambda analysis, so the array literal was analyzed under the invalid expected type `(Int64) -> Unit`/`Unit` chain instead of contributing `Array<Int64>` back to `R`.
- official Cangjie evidence: cjc 1.0.5 accepts `call_inference_03.cj` with no diagnostics and infers `V = Int64`, `R = Array<Int64>`. `external/cangjie_compiler/src/Sema/TypeArgumentInference.cpp:421-510` keeps lambda return constraints in the call inference system and solves them from the lambda result rather than fixing the call result from an enclosing `Unit` statement context.
- Kotlin counterpart files consulted: `external/kotlin/compiler/fir/resolve/src/org/jetbrains/kotlin/fir/resolve/transformers/body/resolve/FirDeclarationsResolveTransformer.kt`; `external/kotlin/compiler/fir/resolve/src/org/jetbrains/kotlin/fir/resolve/inference/FirCallCompleter.kt`; and `external/kotlin/compiler/fir/resolve/src/org/jetbrains/kotlin/fir/resolve/inference/PostponedArgumentsAnalyzer.kt`.
- CFIR owner files changed: `cfir/resolve/src/org/cangnova/cangjie/cfir/resolve/body/CfirDeclarationsResolveTransformer.kt`.
- repair principle: treat the body of a non-anonymous explicitly `Unit` function as a statement context while retaining expected-return propagation for non-`Unit` functions and the call-completion-owned mode for anonymous functions.
- fixtures covered: PSI and non-PSI `call_inference_03.cj`; neighboring guards `call9.cj`, `call10.cj`, `call30.cj`, and `variadic_op_call_1.cj` cover ordinary lambda completion and call resolution behavior.
- fixture correction: none.
- focused verification command: `.\gradlew.bat :cfir:analysis-tests:test --tests '*.llt.call.CallGenerated.testCallInference03' --tests '*.llt.call.CallGenerated.testCall9' --tests '*.llt.call.CallGenerated.testCall10' --tests '*.llt.call.CallGenerated.testCall30' --tests '*.llt.call.CallGenerated.testVariadicOpCall1' -x :cfir:analysis-tests:generateTestGeneratorForCfirAnalysisTestsTests --no-configuration-cache --no-daemon --max-workers=1 --console=plain`.
- focused verification outcome: `BUILD SUCCESSFUL in 3m 43s`; all 10 PSI/LightTree tests passed.
- class verification command: `.\gradlew.bat :cfir:analysis-tests:test --tests '*.llt.call.CallGenerated' -x :cfir:analysis-tests:generateTestGeneratorForCfirAnalysisTestsTests --no-configuration-cache --no-daemon --max-workers=1 --console=plain`.
- class verification outcome: 172 tests completed in 1m14s; failures dropped from 55 to 53, exactly removing PSI/LightTree `call_inference_03` with no new failures in the complete Call slice.

## Instance-member owner substitution is applied exactly once by the substitution scope

- problem type: a generic instance member invoked through a concretely parameterized dispatch receiver must use the receiver-instantiated signature without reinterpreting lexical generic parameters as the member owner's original type parameters.
- root cause: `CfirClassSubstitutionScope` had already produced a substitution override whose parameters and return type were instantiated from the concrete receiver, but `CfirCreateFreshTypeVariableSubstitutorStage` unwrapped that symbol and rebuilt the same owner map from the receiver. Applying the second map to the already substituted signature changed `_05`'s `get(String): outer-T` return into `String` and `_08`'s `apply(outer-V): method-R` parameter into `method-R`.
- official Cangjie evidence: cjc 1.0.5 accepts both complete fixtures with no semantic diagnostics; `_05` also runs with exit code 0. `external/cangjie_compiler/src/Sema/ExtraScopes.cpp:232-329` establishes receiver-to-owner mappings during member access; `TypeManager.cpp:542-559,574-593` builds and propagates generic mappings through inheritance; `TypeCheckCall.cpp:946-1039,1330-1347` keeps lexical context variables rigid, applies the owner mapping to the declaration signature before argument checking, and instantiates the selected result from the same mapping.
- Kotlin counterpart files consulted: `external/kotlin/compiler/fir/providers/src/org/jetbrains/kotlin/fir/scopes/FirKotlinScopeProvider.kt`; `external/kotlin/compiler/fir/providers/src/org/jetbrains/kotlin/fir/scopes/impl/FirClassSubstitutionScope.kt`; and `external/kotlin/compiler/fir/resolve/src/org/jetbrains/kotlin/fir/resolve/calls/stages/CreateFreshTypeVariableSubstitutorStage.kt`.
- CFIR owner files changed: `cfir/resolve/src/org/cangnova/cangjie/cfir/resolve/calls/stages/CfirCreateFreshTypeVariableSubstitutorStage.kt`.
- repair principle: make `CfirClassSubstitutionScope` the single owner of use-site substitution for ordinary non-static instance substitution overrides, while retaining late owner mapping for constructors, enum constructors, static/bare qualifiers, and candidates not produced by a concrete substitution scope.
- fixtures covered: PSI and non-PSI `call_inference_05.cj` and `call_inference_08.cj`; the shared rule covers ordinary instance functions, properties, and field callables, including inherited generic members and signatures containing lexical generic parameters.
- fixture correction: none.
- focused verification command: `.\gradlew.bat :cfir:analysis-tests:test --tests '*.llt.call.CallGenerated.testCallInference05' --tests '*.llt.call.CallGenerated.testCallInference08' -x :cfir:analysis-tests:generateTestGeneratorForCfirAnalysisTestsTests --no-configuration-cache --no-daemon --max-workers=1 --console=plain`.
- focused verification outcome: `BUILD SUCCESSFUL in 3m 21s`; all 4 PSI/LightTree tests passed.
- class verification command: `.\gradlew.bat :cfir:analysis-tests:test --tests '*.llt.call.CallGenerated' -x :cfir:analysis-tests:generateTestGeneratorForCfirAnalysisTestsTests --no-configuration-cache --no-daemon --max-workers=1 --console=plain`.
- class verification outcome: 172 tests completed in 1m7s; failures dropped from 53 to 49, exactly removing PSI/LightTree `call_inference_05` and `_08` with no new failures in the complete Call slice.

## Nested generic arguments retain one candidate and one constraint subsystem

- problem type: a generic call used as an argument must keep its fresh return variable alive until the outer parameter expected type arrives, and the outer constraint must complete the same nested candidate that is stored in the outer argument atom.
- root cause: the initial context-dependent resolution of `infer<T>()` created a partially completed inner candidate and imported its subsystem into the outer candidate. `ArgumentCheckingProcessor.resolveNestedCallForExpectedType` then copied the call and created a second candidate when `Bar` became known. The expected-type constraint was added to the second fresh variable, while full completion still traversed the first atom and fixed its unconstrained variable to `ConeCannotInferGenericFunctionTypeParameterType`.
- official Cangjie evidence: cjc 1.0.5 accepts all four operator/member forms in `call_inference_09.cj` with no semantic diagnostics, while a standalone `let value = infer()` correctly reports `sema_unable_to_infer_generic_func`. `external/cangjie_compiler/src/Sema/TypeCheckCall.cpp:1165-1180,1330-1407,2908-2973` checks an argument under the outer parameter target and applies one substitution pack to the selected nested call; `TypeArgumentInference.cpp:421-458,490-522,531-577` retains the nested return placeholder and diagnoses only after contextual constraints fail to solve it; `LocalTypeArgumentSynthesis.cpp:96-119` adds the contextual return constraint.
- Kotlin counterpart files consulted: `external/kotlin/compiler/fir/resolve/src/org/jetbrains/kotlin/fir/resolve/inference/FirCallCompleter.kt`; `calls/candidate/CandidateFactory.kt`; `calls/stages/CheckArguments.kt`; `calls/stages/ArgumentCheckingProcessor.kt`; `inference/CandidateTraversal.kt`; and the constraint-system completer not-enough-information path.
- CFIR owner files changed: `cfir/resolve/src/org/cangnova/cangjie/cfir/resolve/calls/stages/ArgumentCheckingProcessor.kt`.
- repair principle: when a unique nested function candidate already owns an unfixed variable in its result type, reuse the original atom and subsystem so outer expected-type constraints complete that exact variable; reserve isolated re-resolution for genuine callable ambiguity or candidates whose return no longer depends on their own unfixed variables.
- fixtures covered: PSI and non-PSI `call_inference_09.cj`, including operator calls and ordinary member calls in both expression-body and local-initializer contexts.
