## Record selective dependency topology and declaration-owned metadata lookup

- problem type: LLT fixtures that explicitly depend on another Cangjie source must preserve the official module topology, and every visibility/file/owner query for a dependency declaration must use that declaration symbol's own session/provider rather than the use-site module provider.
- root cause: the LLT service did not have a selective dependency-source directive, so `struct_2.cj` could not express its same-module companion source and `struct_1.cj` could not express a separately compiled dependency module. After the dependency symbol became resolvable, call visibility still queried its container file through the main module session; the main provider does not own the dependency source file index, so the protected constructor was rejected and the resulting error receiver cascaded into unresolved member diagnostics.
- official Cangjie evidence: `cjc 1.0.5` accepts `struct_2.cj` together with `struct_data.cj` in one module, and also accepts `struct_1.cj` when `struct_data.cj` is compiled as a separate dependency library. Both official topologies allow package-related access to the protected `Data` declaration, constructor, function, field, and property. The fixture source text with test directives removed remains identical to `HEAD`.
- Kotlin counterpart files consulted: `external/kotlin/compiler/fir/providers/src/org/jetbrains/kotlin/fir/FirVisibilityChecker.kt`, where `declarationSymbol.moduleData.session.firProvider.getContainingFile(declarationSymbol)` owns the declaration-file lookup; and the FIR provider implementation path that unwraps fake/substitution overrides before querying the original symbol's module provider.
- CFIR owner files changed: `tests/test-infrastructure/testFixtures/org/cangnova/cangjie/test/directives/CfirDiagnosticsDirectives.kt`; `cfir/analysis-tests/testFixtures/org/cangnova/cangjie/cfir/analysis/tests/services/LltCompanionSourceFilesProvider.kt`; `cfir/providers/src/org/cangnova/cangjie/cfir/resolve/providers/CfirProviderUtils.kt`; `cfir/resolve/src/org/cangnova/cangjie/cfir/resolve/calls/visibility/CfirVisibilitySymbolUtils.kt`; `cfir/resolve/src/org/cangnova/cangjie/cfir/resolve/calls/VisibilityUtils.kt`; `cfir/resolve/src/org/cangnova/cangjie/cfir/resolve/body/CfirCallResolver.kt`; `cfir/checkers/src/org/cangnova/cangjie/cfir/analysis/checkers/declaration/CfirConstructorDelegationChecker.kt`; `CfirOverrideCheckerUtils.kt`; `CfirConflictsHelpers.kt`; `analysis/analysis-api-cfir/src/org/cangnova/cangjie/analysis/api/cfir/components/CaCfirVisibilityChecker.kt`; and `tests/test-infrastructure/testFixtures/org/cangnova/cangjie/test/frontend/CfirScopeDumpHandler.kt`.
- repair principle: declaration metadata is normalized to the original symbol and routed exclusively through that symbol's module session; test dependency sources are loaded only when explicitly named and retain module boundaries, so neither compiler resolution nor the LLT harness scans or merges unrelated sibling files.
- fixtures covered: PSI and LightTree `record/struct_1.cj`, `record/struct_2.cj`, and the independent `record/struct_data.cj` guard; existing PSI and LightTree `redefinition/private_func/01_diff_file/{1,2}.cj` and `03_public/{1,2}.cj` guard the pre-existing `LLT_COMPANION_SOURCES` behavior.
- fixture additions and corrections: added module-applicable `DEPENDENCE`; `struct_2.cj` selects `struct_data.cj` in the same module, while `struct_1.cj` declares `data` and `main(data)` modules and attaches `struct_data.cj` only to `data`. No Cangjie grammar or source statement changed, and temporary `SCOPE_DUMP`/overrides investigation artifacts were removed.

## Ordinary assignment RHS expected-type mismatch ownership

- problem type: ordinary `lValue = rValue` must classify and report the RHS expected-type failure at the assignment owner without reusing the VArray-only `ASSIGNMENT_TYPE_MISMATCH`, duplicating literal overflow, or losing the pre-error actual type of calls and composite expressions.
- root cause: the assignment checker recomputed mismatch semantics from the final RHS tree and receiver syntax after body resolve had already replaced some call/array roots with `ConeErrorType`. That erased the actual type, made ordinary assignments depend on expression shape, emitted `ASSIGNMENT_TYPE_MISMATCH` outside the VArray path, and allowed `LITERAL_NUMERIC_OVERFLOW` or `TYPE_INCOMPATIBLE` cascades that the official assignment check does not produce.
- official Cangjie evidence: `external/cangjie_compiler/src/Sema/TypeCheckExpr/AssignExpr.cpp:329-398` performs the ordinary RHS `Check` and keeps VArray/subscript handling separate; `TypeCheckUtil.cpp:228-231` owns the generic mismatch; `TypeCheckExpr/NameReferenceExpr.cpp:68-82` preserves name-reference typing; `TypeCheckExpr/LitConstExpr.cpp:26-125,182-190` distinguishes `boolean`, `integer`, `floating-point`, and `character` conversion failures. `cjc 1.0.5` confirms that ordinary assignments use `sema_mismatched_types`, direct incompatible Bool/Int/Float/Rune literals use only `sema_cannot_convert_literal`, Unit uses only the mismatch, direct String additionally keeps the assignment-level incompatibility, and VArray subscript assignment remains a separate contract.
- Kotlin counterpart files consulted: `external/kotlin/compiler/fir/resolve/src/org/jetbrains/kotlin/fir/resolve/transformers/body/resolve/FirExpressionsResolveTransformer.kt:1424-1474`; `external/kotlin/compiler/fir/resolve/src/org/jetbrains/kotlin/fir/resolve/transformers/body/resolve/BodyResolveContext.kt:93-106`; `external/kotlin/compiler/fir/checkers/src/org/jetbrains/kotlin/fir/analysis/checkers/expression/FirAssignmentTypeMismatchChecker.kt`; and `external/kotlin/compiler/fir/resolve/src/org/jetbrains/kotlin/fir/resolve/transformers/FirCallCompletionResultsWriterTransformer.kt`. Kotlin supplies the assignment-RHS expected-type frame and checker separation; Cangjie-specific diagnostic selection remains governed by the official compiler.
- CFIR owner files changed: `cfir/cfir-tree/src/org/cangnova/cangjie/cfir/expressions/CfirAssignmentTypeMismatchOutcome.kt`; `cfir/cfir-tree/tree-generator/src/org/cangnova/cangjie/cfir/tree/generator/CfirTree.kt`; `Types.kt`; generated `CfirAssignment.kt`, `CfirAssignmentBuilder.kt`, and `CfirAssignmentImpl.kt`; `cfir/resolve/src/org/cangnova/cangjie/cfir/resolve/transformers/body/resolve/BodyResolveContext.kt`; `cfir/resolve/src/org/cangnova/cangjie/cfir/resolve/body/CfirExpressionsResolveTransformer.kt`; `cfir/resolve/src/org/cangnova/cangjie/cfir/resolve/inference/CfirCallCompletionResultsWriterTransformer.kt`; `cfir/checkers/src/org/cangnova/cangjie/cfir/analysis/checkers/expression/CfirAssignmentTypeMismatchChecker.kt`; `CfirLiteralNumericOverflowChecker.kt`; and `cfir/checkers/src/org/cangnova/cangjie/cfir/analysis/diagnostics/CfirTypeSemanticsDiagnostics.kt`.
- repair principle: body resolve records one assignment-local structured outcome while the real RHS owner still holds the actual type, and the assignment checker only renders that outcome; the common `AssignExpr` subtype check covers every valid root, while literal/call/array owners only preserve semantics that would otherwise be destroyed before checker time.
- fixtures covered: PSI and LightTree `llt/assign/assign_003.cj`, `assign_004.cj`, `llt/record/record_vardecl_no_type02.cj`, `llt/users/UsersTest4/UserTest4.cj`, record `primaryConstructor6.cj` and `primaryConstructor7.cj`, class `primaryConstructor11.cj`, and `llt/generics/generic_constraint/generic_constraint12.cj`; diagnostics and diagnostics2 `type-mismatch/assignmentTypeMismatch.cj`; unchanged guards include class `primaryConstructor2.cj` for String plus `TYPE_INCOMPATIBLE`, and `llt/varray/varray_index01.cj` for the independent VArray `ASSIGNMENT_TYPE_MISMATCH` path.
- fixture corrections: ordinary assignment expectations were changed from `ASSIGNMENT_TYPE_MISMATCH` to official `TYPE_MISMATCH`; direct incompatible Bool and out-of-range integer literals were changed to `CANNOT_CONVERT_LITERAL`; `record_vardecl_no_type02.cj` gained the independently required immutable-assignment and RHS mismatch diagnostics. `varray_index01.cj` was intentionally unchanged.

Record drops from six to five failures per entry path because `record_vardecl_no_type02` is now green; the remaining failures are `record_vardecl_check`, `record_argument_unit`, `record_private_init`, `record_3`, and `record_9`.

## Enum pattern target typing and local lambda initializer PCLA boundaries

- problem type: enum pattern constructor access, unreachable enum-pattern diagnostics, and local lambda initializer parameter typing must agree with official enum constructor constraints across PSI and LightTree LLT paths.
- root cause: three EnumPattern fixtures still carried stale expectations for officially unreachable enum patterns, while the remaining `enum_and_var_pattern.cj` failure came from function-value call completion dropping a structural dependency created inside the lambda body. `match (x) { case Some(v) => ... }` correctly constrained the fresh parameter placeholder as `x <: Option<T>`, but `CandidateFactory.boundaryConstraintStorage()` copied only the direct lambda boundary placeholder and discarded the dependent `T`, so the later `f1(Some(3))` call saw a naked placeholder and cascaded into `LAMBDA_MUST_HAVE_TYPE_ANNOTATION`, `AMBIGUOUS_FUNCTION_CALL`, `NO_MATCHING_OPERATOR_INVOKE`, and `GENERIC_TYPE_SHOULD_BE_USED_WITH_TYPE_ARGUMENT`.
- official Cangjie evidence: `cjc 1.0.5` accepts `enum_and_var_pattern.cj` with exit code 0. `cjc 1.0.5` reports `sema_unreachable_pattern` on the second `Year(1)` in `enum02.cj`, on `_` in `enum12.cj`, and on the second `Twounit(year, month)` in `enum13.cj`. `external/cangjie_compiler/src/Sema/TypeCheckPattern.cpp:367-384` routes enum patterns through `FindEnumPatternTarget`, `ConstrainByCtor`, and `SetTyForEnumPatternConstructor`; `external/cangjie_compiler/src/Sema/TypeManager.cpp:2250-2268` builds constructor-shaped placeholder constraints; `external/cangjie_compiler/src/Sema/TypeCheckMatchExpr.cpp:116-120` greedily substitutes unresolved selector placeholders and rechecks patterns.
- Kotlin counterpart files consulted: `external/kotlin/compiler/fir/resolve/src/org/jetbrains/kotlin/fir/resolve/inference/ConstraintSystemCompleter.kt`; `external/kotlin/compiler/fir/resolve/src/org/jetbrains/kotlin/fir/resolve/inference/PostponedArgumentsAnalyzer.kt`; `external/kotlin/compiler/fir/resolve/src/org/jetbrains/kotlin/fir/resolve/calls/ConeResolutionAtoms.kt`.
- CFIR owner files changed: `cfir/resolve/src/org/cangnova/cangjie/cfir/resolve/calls/candidate/CandidateFactory.kt`; `cfir/resolve/src/org/cangnova/cangjie/cfir/resolve/body/CfirExpressionsResolveTransformer.kt`; fixture corrections in `cfir/analysis-tests/testData/llt/PatternMatching/EnumPattern/enum02.cj`, `enum12.cj`, and `enum13.cj`.
- repair principle: enum-pattern-derived selector constraints are constructor-shaped type constraints, so local lambda initializer completion must copy the complete boundary constraint closure rather than only the syntactic parameter placeholder; fixture edits are limited to cases where official `cjc` proves the old expected diagnostics were stale.
- fixtures covered: the full PSI and LightTree `PatternMatching/EnumPattern/EnumPatternGenerated` suites, including `enum_and_var_pattern.cj`, `enum02.cj`, `enum12.cj`, and `enum13.cj`.
- fixture correction: added `UNREACHABLE_PATTERN` to the official unreachable enum-pattern sites in `enum02.cj`, `enum12.cj`, and `enum13.cj`; no Cangjie source statement was changed.
- focused verification command: `.\gradlew.bat :cfir:analysis-tests:test --tests '*EnumPatternGenerated.testEnumAndVarPattern' -x :cfir:analysis-tests:generateTestGeneratorForCfirAnalysisTestsTests --no-configuration-cache --no-daemon --max-workers=1 --console=plain --no-build-cache`.
- focused verification outcome: `BUILD SUCCESSFUL`; XML reported PSI `1 test, 0 failures, 0 errors` and LightTree `1 test, 0 failures, 0 errors`.
- family verification command: `$env:GRADLE_USER_HOME='D:\.gradle-codex-cangjie'; .\gradlew.bat :cfir:analysis-tests:test --tests 'org.cangnova.cangjie.cfir.analysis.tests.CfirAnalysisLLTTest.llt.PatternMatching.EnumPattern.EnumPatternGenerated' --tests 'org.cangnova.cangjie.cfir.analysis.tests.CfirAnalysisLLTPsiTest.llt.PatternMatching.EnumPattern.EnumPatternGenerated' -x :cfir:analysis-tests:generateTestGeneratorForCfirAnalysisTestsTests --no-configuration-cache --no-daemon --max-workers=1 --console=plain --no-build-cache`.

## Invalid expression recovery through operators, member calls, and string interpolation

- problem type: invalid root expressions must propagate a non-reporting error type through later operator calls, member access, member-call arguments, and string interpolation instead of producing secondary `AMBIGUOUS_MATCH_PRIMITIVE_EXTEND`, `UNRESOLVED_REFERENCE`, `NOT_MEMBER_OF`, `INVALID_UNARY_EXPR`, `INVALID_BINARY_OPERATOR`, or invoke diagnostics.
- root cause: CFIR already had `ConeUnreportedDuplicateDiagnostic` but used it only on selected paths. Logical `&&` / `||` were typed as `Bool` even when operand types were invalid, primitive-operator fast resolution ignored `ConeErrorType` operands, member lookup only treated recoverable nominal receiver diagnostics as dominating, call argument resolution still descended into arguments after an explicit receiver had become an error, and string interpolation always wrote `String` even when a part expression was already invalid.
- official Cangjie evidence: `cjc 1.0.5 --diagnostic-format=noColor` on `codex-probes/errmsgs_binary_recovery.cj` reports only the two root invalid `+` diagnostics; `codex-probes/errmsgs_composition_recovery.cj` reports only the root invalid `&&`; `codex-probes/errmsgs_error_value_cascade.cj` reports only the root invalid `+` before later member/unary/binary/invoke uses; `codex-probes/errmsgs_error_callee_arguments.cj` reports the root invalid `+` and the independent generic-argument error, but no `e3` unresolved cascade; `codex-probes/errmsgs_error_callee_argument_root.cj` reports only the receiver root error. `external/cangjie_compiler/src/Sema/TypeCheckExpr/BinaryExpr.cpp:706-840` owns logical/binary operator synthesis and root diagnostics; `TypeCheckExpr/UnaryExpr.cpp:55-95` reports a unary error only when the operand synthesizes a correct type; `TypeCheckCall.cpp:2618-2623` suppresses call diagnostics when invalid argument types were already reported; `TypeCheckExpr/LitConstExpr.cpp:146-175` makes the whole interpolation invalid when any interpolated expression check fails.
- Kotlin counterpart files consulted: `external/kotlin/compiler/fir/resolve/src/org/jetbrains/kotlin/fir/resolve/transformers/body/resolve/FirExpressionsResolveTransformer.kt` for boolean operators and string concatenation traversal, and `external/kotlin/compiler/fir/checkers/src/org/jetbrains/kotlin/fir/analysis/diagnostics/coneDiagnosticToFirDiagnostic.kt:405-458` for suppressing ambiguity diagnostics when call operands already contain error types.
- CFIR owner files changed: `cfir/resolve/src/org/cangnova/cangjie/cfir/resolve/body/CfirExpressionsResolveTransformer.kt`, `cfir/resolve/src/org/cangnova/cangjie/cfir/resolve/body/CfirCallResolver.kt`, and `cfir/checkers/src/org/cangnova/cangjie/cfir/analysis/diagnostics/coneDiagnosticToCfirDiagnostic.kt`.
- repair principle: once a subexpression owns the root semantic error, later expressions keep type-shape information only as delegated type metadata and otherwise propagate a `ConeUnreportedDuplicateDiagnostic`; diagnostics are suppressed only for the same operator/member/call cascade whose operand or receiver already carries an error type.
- fixtures covered: PSI and LightTree `ErrMsgsGenerated.testBinaryExpressions0`, `testComposition0`, `testMemberAccess0`, `testMemberAccess1`, `testUnary0`, and `testStringInterpolation0`.
- fixture correction: none.
- focused verification command: `.\gradlew.bat :cfir:analysis-tests:test --tests '*ErrMsgsGenerated.testStringInterpolation0' --tests '*ErrMsgsGenerated.testMemberAccess1' --tests '*ErrMsgsGenerated.testMemberAccess0' --tests '*ErrMsgsGenerated.testUnary0' --tests '*ErrMsgsGenerated.testBinaryExpressions0' --tests '*ErrMsgsGenerated.testComposition0' -x :cfir:analysis-tests:generateTestGeneratorForCfirAnalysisTestsTests --no-configuration-cache --no-daemon --max-workers=1 --console=plain --no-build-cache`.
- focused verification outcome: `BUILD SUCCESSFUL`; fresh XML reports `12 tests, 0 failures, 0 errors` across PSI and LightTree.
- ErrMsgs regression command: `.\gradlew.bat :cfir:analysis-tests:test --tests 'org.cangnova.cangjie.cfir.analysis.tests.CfirAnalysisLLTTest.llt.ErrMsgs.ErrMsgsGenerated' --tests 'org.cangnova.cangjie.cfir.analysis.tests.CfirAnalysisLLTPsiTest.llt.ErrMsgs.ErrMsgsGenerated' -x :cfir:analysis-tests:generateTestGeneratorForCfirAnalysisTestsTests --no-configuration-cache --no-daemon --max-workers=1 --console=plain --no-build-cache`.
- ErrMsgs regression outcome: XML/console report `84 tests completed, 46 failed`, down from the previous `58` failures. The remaining failures are separate ErrMsgs contracts: declaration/generic constraints, synchronized/try/coalescing/subscript/question-mark/range/spawn handling, assignment/inc-dec, type-argument inference, and diagnostic-bloat pattern reporting.

## Argument-mapping failures terminate call checking and propagate InvalidTy through outer calls

- problem type: ordinary arity and named-argument mapping failures must own one primary call diagnostic, terminate argument/lambda checking for that candidate, and remain semantically invalid through enclosing calls so later member access does not report a derived error.
- root cause: `ArgumentMappingOutcome` recorded mapping failures but argument checking and completion did not consume that state. Error candidates either stopped before the completion writer could finalize nested lambdas or continued with partial mappings, producing argument/lambda cascades. After the immediate failed call became `ConeErrorType`, successful enclosing calls still wrote their normal declaration result types, so `Config(...)` invalidity was lost through `Option.Some(...)` and `App(...)` and the later `.run()` was incorrectly diagnosed as `NOT_MEMBER_OF`.
- official Cangjie evidence: cjc 1.0.5 reports only `sema_wrong_number_of_arguments` for `trailing_closure03.cj`, `trailing_closure06.cj`, and `trailing_closure16.cj`, and only `sema_multiple_named_argument` for `trailing_closure_named_args_02.cj`. `external/cangjie_compiler/src/Sema/TypeCheckCall.cpp:50-74,618-672,1365-1408` terminates candidate checking after ordinary arity or named mapping failure; `TypeCheckCall.cpp:2227-2232` returns `InvalidTy` for a failed call; `TypeCheckCall.cpp:2611-2623` suppresses derived call diagnostics when an argument is already invalid. For `enum6_test.cj`, cjc reports the three independent undeclared-type errors plus only `sema_need_named_argument` at `16:44-81`; an equivalent `Option<Int64>` probe still reports only `sema_need_named_argument`. `external/cangjie_compiler/src/Sema/TypeCheckExpr/NameReferenceExpr.cpp:291-309` does not report a missing member when the base expression has invalid type.
- Kotlin counterpart files consulted: `external/kotlin/compiler/fir/resolve/src/org/jetbrains/kotlin/fir/resolve/transformers/FirCallCompletionResultsWriterTransformer.kt`; `external/kotlin/compiler/fir/resolve/src/org/jetbrains/kotlin/fir/resolve/transformers/body/resolve/FirExpressionsResolveTransformer.kt`; `external/kotlin/compiler/fir/providers/src/org/jetbrains/kotlin/fir/resolve/ScopeUtils.kt`; and `external/kotlin/compiler/fir/providers/src/org/jetbrains/kotlin/fir/resolve/calls/FirReceivers.kt`. Kotlin supplies the error-call writer and phase-ownership architecture; Cangjie-specific `InvalidTy` propagation remains governed by official Cangjie evidence.
- CFIR owner files changed: `cfir/cfir-tree/src/org/cangnova/cangjie/cfir/declarations/CfirLambdaParameterData.kt`; `cfir/resolve/src/org/cangnova/cangjie/cfir/resolve/calls/stages/CfirCheckArguments.kt`; `cfir/resolve/src/org/cangnova/cangjie/cfir/resolve/inference/CfirCallCompleter.kt`; `cfir/resolve/src/org/cangnova/cangjie/cfir/resolve/inference/CfirCallCompletionResultsWriterTransformer.kt`; `cfir/checkers/src/org/cangnova/cangjie/cfir/analysis/checkers/declaration/CfirFunctionLambdaChecker.kt`; `cfir/checkers/src/org/cangnova/cangjie/cfir/analysis/collectors/components/ErrorNodeDiagnosticCollectorComponent.kt`; and `cfir/checkers/src/org/cangnova/cangjie/cfir/analysis/diagnostics/coneDiagnosticToCfirDiagnostic.kt`.
- repair principle: make the structured argument-mapping outcome the shared stage boundary, complete failed-call subtrees only to preserve their primary diagnostic and final tree shape, and propagate an invalid child through every enclosing call while keeping the declaration result solely as delegated recovery metadata; never recover an invalid receiver as a successful nominal member scope.
- fixtures covered: PSI and LightTree `trailing_closure03.cj`, `trailing_closure06.cj`, `trailing_closure16.cj`, `trailing_closure_named_args_02.cj`, and `generics/enum6_test.cj`; guards include `generics/function14_test.cj` and `generics/generic_parameters1.cj`.
- fixture correction: none.
- focused verification command: `.\gradlew.bat :cfir:analysis-tests:test --tests '*lambda.trailing_closure.TrailingClosureGenerated.testTrailingClosure03' --tests '*lambda.trailing_closure.TrailingClosureGenerated.testTrailingClosure06' --tests '*lambda.trailing_closure.TrailingClosureGenerated.testTrailingClosure16' --tests '*lambda.trailing_closure.TrailingClosureGenerated.testTrailingClosureNamedArgs02' --tests '*generics.GenericsGenerated.testEnum6Test' --tests '*generics.GenericsGenerated.testFunction14Test' --tests '*generics.GenericsGenerated.testGenericParameters1' -x :cfir:analysis-tests:generateTestGeneratorForCfirAnalysisTestsTests --no-configuration-cache --no-daemon --max-workers=1 --console=plain --no-build-cache`.
- focused verification outcome: fresh XML at `2026-07-26 16:26:43` reports LightTree `7 tests, 0 failures, 0 errors` and PSI `7 tests, 0 failures, 0 errors`.

All mapping-failure fixtures and three generics guards remain green on both entry paths; the remaining logical failures are exactly `trailing_closure09`, `trailing_closure13`, `trailing_closure15`, `trailing_closure_generic_upper`, `trailing_closure_type_mismatch_01`, and `trailing_closure_type_mismatch_02`, each in PSI and LightTree, and are tracked as separate target-typing/diagnostic families.

## Trailing lambda on a non-function formal reports its dedicated mapping diagnostic

- problem type: a trailing lambda mapped to `Any` or to a generic formal without a function upper bound must report `TRAILING_LAMBDA_CANNOT_USED_FOR_NON_FUNCTION` on the complete lambda instead of degrading to `UNRESOLVED_REFERENCE`, an argument mismatch, or an invalid source range.
- root cause: `CfirMapArguments` already produced structured `TrailingLambdaCannotUsedForNonFunction`, but both `ConeInapplicableCandidateError` conversion and the ordinary/variadic diagnostic replay path lacked a conversion for it. The diagnostic was also absent from the argument-mapping ownership classification, so later argument checks could replace the primary error. PSI and LightTree then fell through different unresolved-reference positioning paths, including reverse ranges on LightTree.
- official Cangjie evidence: cjc 1.0.5 reports exactly one `sema_trailing_lambda_cannot_used_for_non_function` for each fixture. `trailing_closure_type_mismatch_01.cj` reports that a generic type without a function upper bound cannot receive a trailing lambda; `trailing_closure_type_mismatch_02.cj` reports that `Any` is a non-function type. `external/cangjie_compiler/src/Sema/TypeCheckCall.cpp:598-614,1033-1046,1173-1183` performs the dedicated formal-target legality check after mapping and terminates the candidate with this diagnostic.
- Kotlin counterpart files consulted: `external/kotlin/compiler/fir/resolve/src/org/jetbrains/kotlin/fir/resolve/calls/stages/MapArguments.kt`; `external/kotlin/compiler/fir/resolve/src/org/jetbrains/kotlin/fir/resolve/calls/FirCallResolver.kt`; and `external/kotlin/compiler/fir/checkers/src/org/jetbrains/kotlin/fir/analysis/diagnostics/coneDiagnosticToFirDiagnostic.kt`. Kotlin supplies the mapping-diagnostic and cone-conversion ownership layers; the dedicated diagnostic identity and trigger are Cangjie-specific.
- CFIR owner files changed: `cfir/checkers/src/org/cangnova/cangjie/cfir/analysis/diagnostics/coneDiagnosticToCfirDiagnostic.kt`.
- repair principle: preserve the mapper's structured Cangjie diagnostic through every candidate-error rendering path and classify it as the primary mapping failure; use its stored complete trailing-lambda source directly instead of reconstructing a range from the enclosing call.
- fixtures covered: PSI and LightTree `trailing_closure_type_mismatch_01.cj` and `trailing_closure_type_mismatch_02.cj`; `trailing_closure09.cj` is the independent legal-function-target negative guard.
- fixture correction: none.
- verification command: `.\gradlew.bat :cfir:analysis-tests:test --tests '*lambda.trailing_closure.TrailingClosureGenerated.testTrailingClosureTypeMismatch01' --tests '*lambda.trailing_closure.TrailingClosureGenerated.testTrailingClosureTypeMismatch02' --tests '*lambda.trailing_closure.TrailingClosureGenerated.testTrailingClosure09' -x :cfir:analysis-tests:generateTestGeneratorForCfirAnalysisTestsTests --no-configuration-cache --no-daemon --max-workers=1 --console=plain --no-build-cache`.
- verification outcome: fresh PSI and LightTree XML at `2026-07-26 16:35:03` each report `3 tests, 1 failure, 0 errors`; both target fixtures pass on both entry paths (`4/4`). The only remaining failure is the deliberately included `trailing_closure09` negative guard, whose false positive belongs to the separate functional-formal substitution family.

## Trailing-lambda legality is owned by mapped formals, not dispatch receivers

- problem type: a legal trailing lambda on a member function whose last formal is a function type must not be rejected because the member call's dispatch receiver is a non-function class instance.
- root cause: `CfirMapArguments` already mapped the external lambda to the final formal and validated that formal's function shape, but the globally registered `CfirTrailingLambdaChecker` repeated a different check after resolution. It inspected `CfirFunctionCall.explicitReceiver`; for `Select<Unit>().otherwise { ... }` that receiver is `Select<Unit>`, not the `() -> Unit` formal, so the checker reported `TRAILING_LAMBDA_CANNOT_USED_FOR_NON_FUNCTION` over the entire otherwise call.
- official Cangjie evidence: cjc 1.0.5 accepts `trailing_closure09.cj` with no semantic error. `external/cangjie_compiler/src/Sema/TypeCheckCall.cpp:589-614,1029-1053,1165-1183` decides trailing-lambda legality from the mapped formal declaration type and checks the lambda against that type; it never substitutes the dispatch receiver for the formal target.
- Kotlin counterpart files consulted: `external/kotlin/compiler/fir/resolve/src/org/jetbrains/kotlin/fir/resolve/calls/FirArgumentsToParametersMapper.kt:69-112,249-278`, which maps an external lambda to the final formal and does not add a post-resolution dispatch-receiver check.
- CFIR owner files changed: `cfir/checkers/src/org/cangnova/cangjie/cfir/analysis/checkers/CommonExpressionCheckers.kt`.

## Illegal function upper bounds remain available as lambda recovery shape

- problem type: a generic declaration must report that a function type is not a legal upper bound while still using that resolved function shape to type a trailing lambda at a call site.
- root cause: `CfirMapArguments` correctly recognized a type parameter with a declared function upper bound as a valid trailing-lambda formal, but fresh-variable construction intentionally filtered that illegal bound out of normal upper-bound constraints. `ArgumentCheckingProcessor` then saw only an unconstrained fresh type variable, created a lambda atom without the declared parameter shape, and reported `LAMBDA_MUST_HAVE_TYPE_ANNOTATION` on the call-site lambda in addition to the declaration legality error.
- official Cangjie evidence: cjc 1.0.5 reports exactly one `sema_upper_bound_must_be_class_or_interface` on the declared `T` in `trailing_closure_generic_upper.cj` and no call-site lambda diagnostic. `external/cangjie_compiler/src/Sema/PreCheck.cpp:1035-1057` records the illegal-upper-bound state without deleting the resolved bound; `TypeCheckCall.cpp:589-595` continues to classify a generic formal with a function upper bound as a functional trailing-lambda target.
- Kotlin counterpart files consulted: `external/kotlin/compiler/fir/resolve/src/org/jetbrains/kotlin/fir/resolve/calls/stages/CreateFreshTypeVariableSubstitutorStage.kt:224-296` and the FIR postponed-lambda argument checking path. Kotlin supplies the separation between fresh-variable registration, declared-bound constraints, and lambda expected shape; Cangjie-specific bound legality comes from the official compiler.
- CFIR owner files changed: `cfir/resolve/src/org/cangnova/cangjie/cfir/resolve/calls/stages/ArgumentCheckingProcessor.kt`.
- repair principle: keep illegal function bounds out of normal subtype/member constraints, but preserve their already-resolved function parameter and return shape exclusively as lambda recovery metadata; declaration legality and expression target typing are distinct semantic products.
- fixtures covered: PSI and LightTree `trailing_closure_generic_upper.cj`; guards include `trailing_closure09.cj`, `trailing_closure_type_mismatch_01.cj`, and `trailing_closure_type_mismatch_02.cj`.
- fixture correction: none.
- verification command: `.\gradlew.bat :cfir:analysis-tests:test --tests '*lambda.trailing_closure.TrailingClosureGenerated.testTrailingClosureGenericUpper' --tests '*lambda.trailing_closure.TrailingClosureGenerated.testTrailingClosure09' --tests '*lambda.trailing_closure.TrailingClosureGenerated.testTrailingClosureTypeMismatch01' --tests '*lambda.trailing_closure.TrailingClosureGenerated.testTrailingClosureTypeMismatch02' -x :cfir:analysis-tests:generateTestGeneratorForCfirAnalysisTestsTests --no-configuration-cache --no-daemon --max-workers=1 --console=plain --no-build-cache`.
- verification outcome: `BUILD SUCCESSFUL in 4m 10s`; fresh XML at `2026-07-26 16:49:43` reports PSI `4 tests, 0 failures, 0 errors` and LightTree `4 tests, 0 failures, 0 errors`.

## Callable-value trailing lambdas are ordinary positional arguments

- problem type: a trailing lambda passed while invoking a callable value must be mapped to the callable value's final parameter and checked as an ordinary value argument; when that parameter is non-functional, the result is `TYPE_MISMATCH` for the complete lambda, not a declaration-call trailing-lambda diagnostic or an invoke-resolution failure.
- root cause: callable-value identity was inferred independently in arity, variadic, ordinary-argument, and trailing-lambda mapping paths. Direct variables and synthetic function-type `invoke` candidates therefore entered different mappings. After mapping was unified, a lambda against a non-function expected type still wrote `(P...) -> fresh R <: Expected` before its body fixed `R`; the constraint appeared compatible initially and later degraded into a contradiction without structured `ArgumentTypeMismatch`.
- official Cangjie evidence: cjc 1.0.5 reports exactly two `sema_mismatched_types` diagnostics on the two complete `{}` expressions in `trailing_closure13.cj`, each with expected `Int64` and actual `() -> Unit`. `external/cangjie_compiler/src/Sema/TypeCheckCall.cpp:2147-2224` creates positional parameters from `FuncTy.paramTys` for callable-value invocation, and `LambdaExpr.cpp:321-330` synthesizes the lambda's own function type before ordinary mismatch checking when the target is not functional.
- Kotlin counterpart files consulted: `external/kotlin/compiler/fir/resolve/src/org/jetbrains/kotlin/fir/resolve/calls/stages/ArgumentCheckingProcessor.kt:423-561`; `FirCallCompleter.kt:69-127`; and the FIR candidate/call-kind structure. Kotlin keeps callable identity on the candidate and postpones lambda-body completion while preserving a structured argument mismatch.
- CFIR owner files changed: `cfir/resolve/src/org/cangnova/cangjie/cfir/resolve/calls/candidate/Candidate.kt`; `cfir/resolve/src/org/cangnova/cangjie/cfir/resolve/calls/CangjieVariadicUtils.kt`; `cfir/resolve/src/org/cangnova/cangjie/cfir/resolve/calls/stages/CfirMapArguments.kt`; `cfir/resolve/src/org/cangnova/cangjie/cfir/resolve/calls/stages/ArgumentCheckingProcessor.kt`; `cfir/resolve/src/org/cangnova/cangjie/cfir/resolve/inference/PostponedArgumentsAnalyzer.kt`; and `cfir/checkers/src/org/cangnova/cangjie/cfir/analysis/diagnostics/coneDiagnosticToCfirDiagnostic.kt`.
- repair principle: make callable-value identity a candidate invariant consumed by every mapping decision, then check a non-target-typed lambda only after its body has produced the final intrinsic function type; declaration-call syntax rules never replace ordinary callable-value argument typing.
- fixtures covered: PSI and LightTree `trailing_closure13.cj`; the complete `TrailingClosureGenerated` suites; and `generics/enum6_test.cj`, `function14_test.cj`, and `generic_parameters1.cj` as mapping/inference guards.
- fixture correction: none.
- focused verification command: `.\gradlew.bat :cfir:analysis-tests:test --tests '*lambda.trailing_closure.TrailingClosureGenerated.testTrailingClosure01' --tests '*lambda.trailing_closure.TrailingClosureGenerated.testTrailingClosure02' --tests '*lambda.trailing_closure.TrailingClosureGenerated.testTrailingClosure03' --tests '*lambda.trailing_closure.TrailingClosureGenerated.testTrailingClosure06' --tests '*lambda.trailing_closure.TrailingClosureGenerated.testTrailingClosure07' --tests '*lambda.trailing_closure.TrailingClosureGenerated.testTrailingClosure09' --tests '*lambda.trailing_closure.TrailingClosureGenerated.testTrailingClosure13' --tests '*lambda.trailing_closure.TrailingClosureGenerated.testTrailingClosure15' --tests '*lambda.trailing_closure.TrailingClosureGenerated.testTrailingClosureGenericUpper' --tests '*lambda.trailing_closure.TrailingClosureGenerated.testTrailingClosureNesting01' --tests '*lambda.trailing_closure.TrailingClosureGenerated.testTrailingClosureTypeMismatch01' --tests '*lambda.trailing_closure.TrailingClosureGenerated.testTrailingClosureTypeMismatch02' -x :cfir:analysis-tests:generateTestGeneratorForCfirAnalysisTestsTests --no-configuration-cache --no-daemon --max-workers=1 --console=plain --no-build-cache`.
- focused verification outcome: `BUILD SUCCESSFUL in 5m 47s`; fresh XML at `2026-07-26 17:23:05` reports PSI `12 tests, 0 failures, 0 errors` and LightTree `12 tests, 0 failures, 0 errors`.

## LightTree call flattening preserves source ownership

- problem type: LightTree construction of a call with a trailing lambda must never assign a call source whose end precedes its start; a parser-generated inner `CALL_EXPRESSION` owns the call suffix only when it directly contains a value-argument or type-argument list.
- root cause: `LightTreeRawCfirExpressionBuilder.convertCall` conflated callee unwrapping, argument inheritance, and source ownership. For simple `x {}`, a degenerate inner call with no suffix became the effective source owner and was then trimmed using the outer lambda offset, producing the invalid range `(50, 48)`.
- official Cangjie evidence: this is a range-only structural defect governed by the repository Diagnostic Range Policy. The complete `{}` is the relevant token range for the official mismatch; no diagnostic layer may clamp a malformed CFIR source.
- Kotlin counterpart files consulted: `external/kotlin/compiler/fir/raw-fir/light-tree2fir/src/org/jetbrains/kotlin/fir/lightTree/LightTreeRawFirExpressionBuilder.kt:741-850`, where each real call suffix owns its source and no degenerate nested call is promoted merely because a trailing lambda exists.
- CFIR owner files changed: `cfir/raw-cfir/light-tree2cfir/src/org/cangnova/cangjie/cfir/lightTree/LightTreeRawCfirExpressionBuilder.kt`.
- repair principle: separate callee unwrapping from call-suffix/source ownership; only a nested node with a real value/type argument list may be flattened as the source owner, and trimming asserts that the trailing lambda lies inside that owner.
- fixtures covered: LightTree and PSI `trailing_closure13.cj`; source guards `trailing_closure01/02/03/06/07/09.cj`, `trailing_closure_nesting_01.cj`, and `trailing_closure_type_mismatch_01/_02.cj`; the complete `TrailingClosureGenerated` suites.
- fixture correction: none.
- verification command and outcome: the focused and family commands above report respectively `24 tests, 0 failures, 0 errors` and `42 tests, 0 failures, 0 errors`; no `FileAnalysisException` or invalid source range remains.

## Error candidates with successful mapping still complete lambda bodies

- problem type: a lambda parameter-shape mismatch makes the candidate erroneous but must not prevent the same lambda body from receiving the mapped function target; nested lambdas continue target-typed analysis, while only the complete outer lambda reports the root mismatch.
- root cause: `CfirCallCompleter.completeCall` returned immediately for every error reference. A candidate with successful argument mapping and `LambdaParameterTypeMismatch` therefore retained reliable formal targets but never completed postponed arguments; `trailing_closure15.cj` lost the inner `(Int64) -> Int64` context and reported `LAMBDA_MUST_HAVE_TYPE_ANNOTATION` on the nested lambda.
- official Cangjie evidence: cjc 1.0.5 reports exactly one `sema_mismatched_types` on the complete outer `{i: Int8 => test {i => i}}`, expected `(Int64) -> Int64` and actual `(Int8) -> (Int64) -> Int64`. `external/cangjie_compiler/src/Sema/TypeCheckExpr/LambdaExpr.cpp:311-374` explicitly states that lambda-body checking must not be short-circuited when parameter matching fails.
- Kotlin counterpart files consulted: `external/kotlin/compiler/fir/resolve/src/org/jetbrains/kotlin/fir/resolve/inference/FirCallCompleter.kt:69-127`; `FirCallCompletionResultsWriterTransformer.kt:842-884`; and the postponed lambda analyzer path.
- CFIR owner files changed: `cfir/resolve/src/org/cangnova/cangjie/cfir/resolve/inference/CfirCallCompleter.kt`; `cfir/resolve/src/org/cangnova/cangjie/cfir/resolve/calls/stages/ArgumentCheckingProcessor.kt`; and `cfir/resolve/src/org/cangnova/cangjie/cfir/resolve/inference/PostponedArgumentsAnalyzer.kt`.
- repair principle: mapping failure, missing mapping, and mapping-successful error candidates are distinct completion states; only the last retains enough formal structure to complete postponed lambda bodies while preserving the original error reference.
- fixtures covered: PSI and LightTree `trailing_closure15.cj`; the complete `TrailingClosureGenerated` suites and three generics guards.
- fixture correction: none.
- verification command and outcome: the focused and family commands above report respectively `24 tests, 0 failures, 0 errors` and `42 tests, 0 failures, 0 errors`; the nested lambda has no annotation diagnostic and the complete outer lambda retains its single `TYPE_MISMATCH`.

## Generic override constraints use their established diagnostic owners

Current CFIR already reports identical correct ranges through both raw entry paths; changing either checker would regress the previously verified Class, ConstraintCheck, Call, Generics, and TrailingClosure families.

## Generic override constraints aggregate parent contracts and preserve transitive generic bounds

- problem type: generic override/redef members must compare each child type-parameter constraint once against the complete inherited contract: bounds inside one declaration form an intersection, while equivalent members inherited from different parents contribute a union of parent domains.
- root cause: `CfirOverrideChecker.checkGenericConstraintCompatibility` iterated parents independently and flattened each declaration's bounds into unrelated entries. That made a single `A & B` parent constraint look stricter than its effective intersection, lost multi-parent aggregation, and lacked the generic-bound transitive closure required for `T1 <: T2 <: T3 <: T4`. An intermediate repair incorrectly removed illegal upper bounds from the comparison; official Cangjie reports their legality error but deliberately retains them in the override constraint graph.
- official Cangjie evidence: cjc 1.0.5 reports D/E and accepts F in `generic_constraint_inheritance_02/03`, reports only D in `_07`, retains `GENERIC_CONSTRAINT_NOT_LOOSER` in `generic_upper_constraint_inheritance_01/03`, and reports no such diagnostic in `generic_upper_constraint_inheritance_04/05`. `external/cangjie_compiler/src/Sema/InheritanceChecker/MergeInheritedMemberHelper.cpp:29-63,135-180,213-227` maps and unions same-signature parent bounds; `StructInheritanceChecker.cpp:234-270,1214-1267` retains generic bounds, constructs a per-declaration intersection, and applies the two-part looser comparison; `PreCheck.cpp:1035-1060,1100-1130` diagnoses illegal bounds without deleting them.
- Kotlin counterpart files consulted: `external/kotlin/compiler/fir/providers/src/org/jetbrains/kotlin/fir/scopes/impl/FirTypeIntersectionScopeContext.kt:209-240,337-365`; `external/kotlin/compiler/fir/tree/src/org/jetbrains/kotlin/fir/symbols/impl/FirFunctionSymbol.kt:66-81`; `external/kotlin/compiler/fir/checkers/src/org/jetbrains/kotlin/fir/analysis/checkers/declaration/FirOverrideChecker.kt:367-375`. Kotlin supplies the complete direct-override/intersection-symbol data flow; Cangjie semantics come from the official compiler.
- CFIR owner files changed: `cfir/checkers/src/org/cangnova/cangjie/cfir/analysis/checkers/declaration/CfirOverrideChecker.kt`; fixture corrections in `generic_constraint_inheritance_02.cj`, `_03.cj`, `_07.cj`, `generic_upper_constraint_inheritance_04.cj`, and `_05.cj`.
- repair principle: build one cycle-safe direct-and-transitive effective intersection per declaration, substitute every parent into the child callable domain, retain the cross-parent union, and run the official two-part comparison once per child type parameter.
- fixtures covered: PSI and LightTree `generic_constraint_inheritance_01.cj` through `_08.cj` and `generic_upper_constraint_inheritance_01.cj` through `_11.cj`; the focused semantic fixtures are `_02`, `_03`, `_07`, `generic_upper_01`, `_03`, `_04`, and `_05`.
- fixture correction: `_02/_03` now expect the cjc-proven D/E diagnostics and accept F; `_07` uses the complete constraint diagnostic owner; `generic_upper_04/_05` retain only the official upper-bound legality diagnostics on complete type-parameter tokens.
- verification command: `$env:JAVA_HOME='C:\Users\lin17\.jdks\corretto-21.0.10'; .\gradlew.bat :cfir:analysis-tests:test --tests '*generic_constraint_inheritance.GenericConstraintInheritanceGenerated' -x :cfir:analysis-tests:generateTestGeneratorForCfirAnalysisTestsTests --no-configuration-cache --no-daemon --max-workers=1 --console=plain --no-build-cache`.
- verification outcome: after the aggregation/closure repair, fresh XML reduced the complete PSI and LightTree suites from `40 tests, 16 failures` to the three independent logical failures `_06/_09/_10` on both entry paths; `_01/_02/_03/_04/_05/_07` and all other guards passed.

## Generic upper-bound member conflicts use use-site members and official diagnostic precedence

- problem type: generic upper-bound intersections must include visible extend members, choose the most specific class bound, report incompatible inherited member types on the complete constraint, and suppress a weaker visibility diagnostic when the same implicit implementation already has an incompatible return type.
- root cause: `CfirTypeParameterBoundsChecker` correctly selected the most specific class bound but created a `DECLARATION_SITE` member scope, which intentionally excludes extend members; `_09/_10` therefore never saw `extend C/B.foo`. Separately, `CfirInheritanceDeepChecker` computed a return-type conflict and then independently emitted `CANNOT_WEAKEN_ACCESS_PRIVILEGE` for the same implicit `S.foo`/`I2.foo` relation.
- official Cangjie evidence: cjc 1.0.5 reports exactly two upper-bound legality errors plus `sema_return_type_incompatible` for `generic_upper_constraint_inheritance_06`, without weak visibility; when the return type is made compatible it reports weak visibility instead. It reports exactly one `sema_inherit_member_type_inconsistent` on the complete constraint in `_09/_10` and no `x.foo()` cascade. `external/cangjie_compiler/src/Sema/InheritanceChecker/StructInheritanceChecker.cpp:1174-1201,1353-1407` establishes return/visibility precedence; `GenericInheritanceChecker.cpp:23-95`, `StructInheritanceChecker.cpp:447-471`, and `PreCheck.cpp:928-951` select the smallest class and merge its visible extend members with interface members.
- Kotlin counterpart files consulted: `external/kotlin/compiler/fir/checkers/src/org/jetbrains/kotlin/fir/analysis/checkers/declaration/FirOverrideChecker.kt:436-466`; `FirImplementationMismatchChecker.kt:80-120`; `external/kotlin/compiler/fir/resolve/src/org/jetbrains/kotlin/fir/resolve/scopes/ScopeUtils.kt:100-121`; and FIR type-intersection scope/context implementations. Kotlin supplies checker ownership and use-site intersection scope structure only.
- CFIR owner files changed: `cfir/checkers/src/org/cangnova/cangjie/cfir/analysis/checkers/declaration/CfirInheritanceDeepChecker.kt`; `CfirTypeParameterBoundsChecker.kt`; fixture correction in `generic_upper_constraint_inheritance_06.cj`.
- repair principle: reuse the canonical `USE_SITE` class member scope so extend participation follows the same accessibility/substitution machinery as normal resolution, and compute one implicit implementation relation whose return conflict takes precedence over its weak-visibility consequence.
- fixtures covered: PSI and LightTree `generic_upper_constraint_inheritance_06.cj`, `_09.cj`, and `_10.cj`, guarded by the complete 19-file `GenericConstraintInheritanceGenerated` family.
- fixture correction: `_06` now expects `RETURN_TYPE_INCOMPATIBLE` on the complete `foo` token and the two `UPPER_BOUND_MUST_BE_CLASS_OR_INTERFACE` diagnostics on the complete generic-parameter `T` tokens; the obsolete weak-visibility expectation is absent.

## Hide compile-time visibility is parser-owned annotation metadata

- problem type: ordinary `@Hide` is compile-time invisible and must report `HIDE_COMPILE_TIME_INVISIBLE`, while `@!Hide` is compile-time visible; ordinary function parameters report `HIDE_AT_FUNC_PARAM` first and do not continue into Hide visibility or argument checks.
- root cause: CFIR discarded the parser-owned `@`/`@!` semantic bit and later guessed visibility from annotation type resolution, so a successfully resolved ordinary `@Hide` looked visible. Macro-expression construction could also replace the annotation source with its input declaration range, preventing the checker from owning the complete annotation token precisely.
- official Cangjie evidence: `external/cangjie_compiler/src/Parse/ParseAnnotations.cpp:236-257` sets `Annotation::isCompileTimeVisible` only for `AT_EXCL`; `external/cangjie_compiler/include/cangjie/AST/Node.h:567-593` stores that bit on the annotation node; `external/cangjie_compiler/src/Sema/Plugin/PluginCustomAnnoChecker.cpp:478-496` gives ordinary function parameters `sema_hide_at_func_param` precedence with `continue`, otherwise reports `sema_hide_compile_time_invisible` on the complete annotation when the bit is false. `ASTWriter.cpp:1642-1735` and `ASTLoader.cpp:630-666` preserve compile-time-visible annotations across module serialization.
- Kotlin counterpart files consulted: `external/kotlin/compiler/fir/tree/gen/org/jetbrains/kotlin/fir/expressions/FirAnnotationCall.kt`; `external/kotlin/compiler/fir/tree/gen/org/jetbrains/kotlin/fir/expressions/impl/FirAnnotationCallImpl.kt`. FIR keeps source and resolution metadata on the annotation call itself; CFIR's stable equivalent across macro splice is the session annotation-slot snapshot and registry.
- CFIR owner files changed: `cfir/providers/src/org/cangnova/cangjie/cfir/resolve/providers/macro/CfirAnnotationMetadataRegistry.kt`; `cfir/raw-cfir/psi2cfir/src/org/cangnova/cangjie/cfir/builder/PsiRawCfirBuilder.kt`; `cfir/raw-cfir/light-tree2cfir/src/org/cangnova/cangjie/cfir/lightTree/LightTreeRawCfirDeclarationBuilder.kt`; `compiler/frontend/src/org/cangnova/cangjie/frontend/pipeline/MacroExpandPhase.kt`; `cfir/checkers/src/org/cangnova/cangjie/cfir/analysis/checkers/declaration/CfirBuiltInAnnotationSemanticsChecker.kt`.
- repair principle: capture compile-time visibility and the complete annotation source once at every raw-builder annotation slot, preserve both through macro cache/splice identity migration, and make semantic checkers consume that metadata instead of re-deriving it from resolved types or source text.
- fixtures covered: PSI and LightTree `macro/llt/APILevelChecker/hide/hide_without_compilerTimeVisible.cj`; all five `CfirAnnotationSlotSnapshot` construction paths were statically audited.
- fixture correction: moved `HIDE_COMPILE_TIME_INVISIBLE` from `Hide[isChecked: true]` to the complete `@Hide[isChecked: true]` annotation. Fresh PSI and LightTree XML had identical diagnostic identity and differed only by this range; the complete annotation follows the repository Diagnostic Range Policy and the official checker owner.
- verification command: `$env:JAVA_HOME='C:\Users\lin17\.jdks\corretto-21.0.10'; .\gradlew.bat :cfir:analysis-tests:test --tests '*HideGenerated.testHideWithoutCompilerTimeVisible' -x :cfir:analysis-tests:generateTestGeneratorForCfirAnalysisTestsTests --no-daemon --max-workers=1 --console=plain --no-build-cache --no-configuration-cache`.
